## ⚠️ Quick Safety Reminders

- **Small parts**: dried beans, pasta, lolly sticks, salt dough bricks. *Both* children can choke on these. Count them out and count them back in.
- **String and cord**: the zip line and the wrecking ball both use it. Take it down the moment the activity ends. Never leave a hanging loop within reach.
- **Adult-only steps**: craft knives, scissors, the oven. Look for the adult-only mark in each activity.
- **Salt dough is not food.** It is dangerous if eaten, especially for Kreston and for any dog in the house.
- **Winter specific**: check for ice before outdoor play, and keep outings short below freezing. Both kids lose heat faster than you do.

## Week 1: Shape Engineers & Building 🏗️
**📅 January 1 – January 7, 2027**

---

### 🎯 What This Week Is About

This week she builds towers, bridges, houses and forts. Each day you put the
same question in front of her in a new shape: **what makes a structure stand
up, and what makes it fall down?** She knocks most of it over, rebuilds it
steadier, and runs that loop seven times.

This is also the first week of the year, so on Day 1 you seal the time capsule
you open on Day 365, start the growth chart on the wall, and hang the first of
twelve handprints. Those three threads run to December, and the rest of the
week runs on cardboard.

---

### 🧒 What Azlyn Will Learn

- **Structures need a base.** Wide bottoms hold, narrow ones topple.
- **Shape names in use** (circle, square, triangle), attached to the boxes and cups in her hands.
- **Trial and error as a method.** Build, watch it fail, change one thing, build again.
- **Hand strength and control** through stacking, taping and pressing.
- **That she can make a real thing** that stays in the house and that you use.

---

### 👩 What You'll Learn

- Why writing down her exact words matters more than you'd think
- The cardboard box beats the expensive toy, and there's a reason
- Narrating beats quizzing, every time
- Knocking it down *is* the science
- Why kneading dough calms a wound-up kid down
- The pause before you help
- Why small enclosed spaces are so appealing right now

---

## 🌟 Day 1: The Tower and the Time Capsule 🌟
**📅 Friday, January 1, 2027**
**Theme:** Shape Engineers & Building

### 🌙 Prep Tonight
> Empty the recycling onto the kitchen table (boxes, tubes, cups) and put the shoebox, the paints and a stack of newspaper beside it.

### ⏰ At-a-Glance Schedule
- **8:00–9:00 AM** — 🌅 Opening Activity: Sorting the Build Box
- **9:00–9:15 AM** — Morning Meeting (15 min)
- **9:15–10:15 AM** — 🎨 The Main Event: The Tallest Tower That Stands (60 min)
- **10:15–10:30 AM** — Snack (15 min)
- **10:30–11:15 AM** — 🌳 Get Outside: First Walk of the Year (45 min)
- **11:15–11:45 AM** — Free Play (30 min)
- **11:45 AM–12:00 PM** — 🤸 Wiggle Time (15 min)
- **12:00–12:30 PM** — Lunch
- **12:30–1:00 PM** — 📚 Book Time
- **1:00–2:30 PM** — 🎨 Second Main Event: The Time Capsule (90 min)
- **2:30–3:30 PM** — 🌳 Out Again: **Pick one** (60 min)
- **3:30–4:00 PM** — Snack & Free Play
- **4:00–4:15 PM** — 🧹 Her Job: The Forks
- **4:15–5:00 PM** — Quiet Play: The Cardboard Pile
- **5:00–5:45 PM** — 🛁 Wind-Down: The First Book of the Year
- **6:00 PM** — **Dinner**

### 🌅 Opening Activity: Sorting the Build Box

**🧰 You need:** the recycling pile · 3 paper bags or empty boxes · masking tape.

1. Tip the pile into the middle of the floor and sit down in it with her.
2. Give her three bags and three words: **boxes**, **tubes**, **cups**. Let her sort. She will argue about the odd ones, so let the argument run.
3. Ask which shape she thinks will hold the most weight. Do not answer. Tell her you will find out at 9:15 and leave the question hanging.

### 🎨 The Main Event: The Tallest Tower That Stands

**🧰 Complete Materials List:**
- The sorted boxes, tubes and cups
- 1 roll masking tape
- 1 measuring tape or a length of string
- 1 permanent marker

**📝 Step-by-Step Instructions:**
1. Build a tower as tall as you can with no tape. Let her place every piece. Do not correct the wobbly ones.
2. It will fall. Say *what could we change?* instead of *that was too narrow,* and wait for her answer even when it takes a while.
3. Build again, this time widest boxes at the bottom. Ask her which is stronger and let her push it to find out.
4. Measure the tallest one against her. Mark the height on the wall with a pencil and write **TOWER, JAN 1** beside it.
5. Now hand her the tape and let her build a third one however she likes. Leave her to discover what tape lets her get away with.
6. Leave the best tower standing. She will come back to it at 4:15.

> 💡 **Tip:** Knocking it down is the measurement, so let her do it. A tower that stays up tells her nothing about why the others fell.

### 🌳 Get Outside: First Walk of the Year

Bundle up and walk the route you will walk all year. Look at the trees today,
bare and sleeping, all structure and no leaves, the same thing she has been
stacking all morning. Hand her a paper bag and one instruction: find one thing
worth keeping. It goes in the capsule this afternoon, so tell her that now.

> 👶 **Infant Integration:** Kreston is 6 months and sits with his hands free.
> Give him his own cardboard tube and one paper cup on the floor near the
> building. He will bang, mouth and drop them, running the same investigation
> Azlyn is running with the tower. On the walk, wear him facing out. He is
> alert enough now to take in the street, and cold air on his cheeks is a new
> sensation. Name three things you pass out loud.

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

### 🎨 Second Main Event: The Time Capsule

Morning was tall, loud and falling over. This afternoon you seal a box and put
it away for a year.

**🧰 Complete Materials List:**
- 1 shoebox with a lid
- Thick card, 2 sheets
- Washable paint and a wide brush
- 1 wet flannel in a shallow bowl
- 1 pencil, 1 permanent marker
- 1 measuring tape
- Parcel tape
- Today's walk treasure

**📝 Step-by-Step Instructions:**
1. Paint her palm with the brush rather than dipping her hand. Press flat, count to three, lift straight up. Write **January** and **3** beside it.
2. Do Kreston's on the same sheet while he is calm, flannel already in your hand.
3. Stand them both against the door frame. Mark, date and write the names. Draw round Azlyn's flat hand next to her mark. Do everyone in the house so she can see the row.
4. Ask her four questions and write the answers **word for word**: favourite colour, favourite food, favourite song, what she wants to do this year. Do not tidy her grammar. Her wrong words are the ones you will want to read next December.
5. Let her draw the family. Whatever comes out goes in.
6. Load the box: the drawing, her answers, the walk treasure, a photo. Photograph everything first. Boxes go missing, and the photos on your phone will still be there.
7. Tape it shut, write **OPEN DECEMBER 31, 2027** across the lid, and put it somewhere she can see and not reach.

> 💡 **Tip:** Let her shake it once it is sealed. Tell her it stays shut for a year, and let her sit with how long a year sounds.

### 🎯 Afternoon Alternatives
- Handprints only. Skip the box and do the rest tomorrow.
- Trace round her whole body on the back of a flattened box instead.
- Sort the walk treasure by colour, then by size.
- Look through last year's photos and name who is in each one.

### 🌳 Out Again: **Pick one**
- A friend's house, if anyone is up for a visitor on New Year's Day
- The park, emptier today than any other day this year
- The same walk again in fading light. Ask what is different; the answer is usually the sound
- Drive somewhere with a view and stay in the warm car
- Post a letter to Azlyn for her to open when she is five

> 👶 **Kreston's Afternoon:** He goes on the growth chart too, lying down.
> Mark and date his length on the same wall as everyone else. After that, give
> him the flattened box to lie on. At six months he will stay with the crackle
> and the give under his hands longer than he stays with a toy, and down there
> he is at floor level with Azlyn while she works.

### 🌍 Around the World: Mongolia, the Arctic & the American Southwest 🏠

Houses come in shapes other than boxes. Mongolian families build round
felt **gers** they pack up and move with the seasons. Inuit builders cut snow
blocks and lay them in a spiral to close an **igloo**. In the American
Southwest, builders shape **adobe** homes from mud brick that keeps the
inside cool through the heat.

> **✨ Try it:** Swap the square house for a round one. A blanket over a
> laundry basket makes a good ger. Ask Azlyn which shape feels stronger.

> *Did you know?* Mongolian gers always face south, toward the sun.

---

> 🧠 **A Little Parenting Insight**
> **Why writing down her exact words matters more than you'd think.**
> Azlyn is in the fastest language stretch of her life, adding something like
> ten new words a day. Her mistakes are evidence of rule-building. She says *I
> goed outside* because she has worked out that adding *-ed* makes a thing
> past tense, and she applies that rule to the verbs she knows. Linguists call
> it overregularization, and it marks sophisticated thinking. You do not need
> to correct her. Feed the right form back in your next sentence (*you went
> outside? what did you see?*) and she picks up the exception on her own
> timeline.

> ⚠️ **Safety:** Keep tape rolls, lolly sticks and walk treasure out of
> Kreston's reach the moment you stop watching him. Build the tower away from
> where he is sitting. Azlyn can step back when a cardboard tower comes down
> and Kreston cannot move out of the way. Washable non-toxic paint only.

## 🌟 Day 2: The Box House 🌟
**📅 Saturday, January 2, 2027**
**Theme:** Shape Engineers & Building

### 🌙 Prep Tonight
> Stand the biggest box you own upright in the middle of the living room and leave it there overnight, so she meets it before you do.

### ⏰ At-a-Glance Schedule
- **8:00–9:00 AM** — 🌅 Opening Activity: Choosing the Box
- **9:00–9:15 AM** — Morning Meeting (15 min)
- **9:15–10:15 AM** — 🎨 The Main Event: The Box House (60 min)
- **10:15–10:30 AM** — Snack (15 min)
- **10:30–11:15 AM** — 🌳 Get Outside: The Shape Hunt (45 min)
- **11:15–11:45 AM** — Free Play (30 min)
- **11:45 AM–12:00 PM** — 🤸 Wiggle Time (15 min)
- **12:00–12:30 PM** — Lunch
- **12:30–1:00 PM** — 📚 Book Time
- **1:00–2:30 PM** — 🎨 Second Main Event: Furnishing the Box House (90 min)
- **2:30–3:30 PM** — 🌳 Out Again: **Pick one** (60 min)
- **3:30–4:00 PM** — Snack & Free Play
- **4:00–4:15 PM** — 🧹 Her Job: The Forks
- **4:15–5:00 PM** — Quiet Play: Inside the House
- **5:00–5:45 PM** — 🛁 Wind-Down: Reading in the House
- **6:00 PM** — **Dinner**

### 🌅 Opening Activity: Choosing the Box

**🧰 You need:** the big box · masking tape · 1 permanent marker.

1. Let her climb in before anything else happens. She will do this anyway.
2. Ask what it should be: house, shop, boat, rocket. Write her answer on the side in marker. The decision is now fixed and it is hers.
3. Give her the tape and let her mark where the door and the windows go. She decides now, and you cut along her marks at 9:15.

### 🎨 The Main Event: The Box House

**🧰 Complete Materials List:**
- 1 large box, tall enough for her to sit up in
- 1 craft knife or heavy scissors (**adult only**)
- 1 roll masking tape
- 1 permanent marker
- Offcut card and small boxes

**📝 Step-by-Step Instructions:**
1. Cut the door on three sides so it swings open. Do this yourself with her standing back, and say why she is standing back.
2. Cut two windows where she taped. Follow her marks even when they are in daft places.
3. Hand her the offcuts and the tape and let her build a roof. It will be crooked and it will hold.
4. Give her the marker. Every surface, inside and out, is hers to draw on.
5. Write a house number on the front and let her choose the number.
6. Get in with her. Sit down on the floor inside it and let her show you round.

> 💡 **Tip:** Cut less than you think. You can cut more later, and a box with three walls left standing survives the whole week. A box cut into flaps lasts an afternoon.

### 🌳 Get Outside: The Shape Hunt

Walk the street looking for the shapes she sorted yesterday. Windows are
rectangles, drain covers are circles, road signs are triangles, gates are made
of squares. Ask her to find you three circles before you turn back. Say so out
loud when she finds one you didn't see.

> 👶 **Infant Integration:** Kreston is 6 months. Sit him inside the box house
> on a folded blanket while Azlyn draws on the walls around him. The light and
> the sound both change in there, and he will turn his head hunting for the
> difference. Put him in the middle of her project instead of beside it. Keep
> the door flap propped open so you can see him the whole time.

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

### 🎨 Second Main Event: Furnishing the Box House

She moves somebody in this afternoon. Small boxes, cups and tape turn into
the furniture.

**🧰 Complete Materials List:**
- The box house
- 6–10 small boxes, cups and tubes
- 1 roll masking tape
- 1 scrap of fabric or an old tea towel
- 1 permanent marker
- Crayons or paint

**📝 Step-by-Step Instructions:**
1. Ask what a house needs. Take her list at face value even when it is a bed, a cake and a dog.
2. Make the bed first: a shoebox with the fabric folded into it. Let her decide where it goes.
3. Turn a tall box on its end for a table, cups upside down for stools.
4. Cut a rectangle of card and tape it inside as a picture. She draws whatever hangs in it.
5. Make one thing from her list that you would not have thought of. Follow her design down to the parts you would fix.
6. Move a doll or a teddy in. Give it a name and write the name on the door.
7. Turn the big light off and give her the torch. The cardboard glows and she will not want to come out.

> 💡 **Tip:** Resist tidying the furniture into sensible positions. A stool on the roof is a decision, and if you move it she will stop making decisions.

### 🎯 Afternoon Alternatives
- Throw one blanket over one box and stop there.
- Draw the house you'd like to live in on a flattened box instead.
- Post things through the windows and fish them back out.
- Cut a big cardboard door for the *real* doorway and pretend the house is the room.

### 🌳 Out Again: **Pick one**
- **The museum**, first Saturday of the month and the year's first
- The park, to run off whatever the box didn't
- Soft play if it's raining sideways
- A café with a window seat and something to look at
- The hardware shop to look at boxes, timber and things that stack

> 👶 **Kreston's Afternoon:** Give him one of the small furniture boxes to
> handle before you tape it down. At 6 months he passes objects hand to hand and
> turns them over to look at the other side, both new this month, so watch his
> hands. Let him keep one box for himself, out of the build.

---

> 🧠 **A Little Parenting Insight**
> **The cardboard box beats the expensive toy, and there's a reason.**
> A toy fire engine can be a fire engine. A box can be a fire engine, a house,
> a boat, a bed, and a hiding place, sometimes inside ten minutes. Researchers
> who study play call that open-endedness, and children play longer and talk
> more with materials that have not decided what they are. A box also fails on
> her. It collapses, tears and needs mending, and each of those is a problem
> she gets to solve with tape while the fire engine sits there working. Hand
> her the packaging instead of what came in it and you have handed her the
> better object.

> ⚠️ **Safety:** The craft knife is adult-only. Put it back out of reach
> between cuts, on a shelf and not the arm of the sofa. Check inside the box for
> staples before either child gets in. Never leave Kreston in the box house
> alone, and keep the door flap open the whole time he is in it.

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

## 🌟 Day 3: Ramps and Bridges 🌟
**📅 Sunday, January 3, 2027**
**Theme:** Shape Engineers & Building

### 🌙 Prep Tonight
> Save every cardboard tube in the house and put them by the stairs with the masking tape and a bag of dried beans.

### ⏰ At-a-Glance Schedule
- **8:00–9:00 AM** — 🌅 Opening Activity: Taping the Tubes
- **9:00–9:15 AM** — Morning Meeting (15 min)
- **9:15–10:15 AM** — 🎨 The Main Event: The Ball Run (60 min)
- **10:15–10:30 AM** — Snack (15 min)
- **10:30–11:15 AM** — 🌳 Get Outside: Stick Bridges (45 min)
- **11:15–11:45 AM** — Free Play (30 min)
- **11:45 AM–12:00 PM** — 🤸 Wiggle Time (15 min)
- **12:00–12:30 PM** — Lunch
- **12:30–1:00 PM** — 📚 Book Time
- **1:00–2:30 PM** — 🎨 Second Main Event: The Bridge That Holds Teddy (90 min)
- **2:30–3:30 PM** — 🌳 Out Again: **Pick one** (60 min)
- **3:30–4:00 PM** — Snack & Free Play
- **4:00–4:15 PM** — 🧹 Her Job: The Forks
- **4:15–5:00 PM** — Quiet Play: The Bean Jar
- **5:00–5:45 PM** — 🛁 Wind-Down: Warm Bath, Long Books
- **6:00 PM** — **Dinner**

### 🌅 Opening Activity: Taping the Tubes

**🧰 You need:** cardboard tubes · masking tape · scissors · a smooth wall or the side of a wardrobe.

1. Cut two or three tubes in half lengthways so they become open channels. You cut, she holds.
2. Tape one channel to the wall at a slope. Roll a dried bean down it and watch where it lands.
3. Ask her where the next one should go so the bean keeps travelling. Tape it exactly where she says, even if it will not work.

### 🎨 The Main Event: The Ball Run

**🧰 Complete Materials List:**
- 6–10 cardboard tubes, some cut open
- 1 roll masking tape
- Dried beans, dried pasta, a ping-pong ball if you have one
- 1 shallow box or saucepan for the bottom
- A wall or wardrobe side you don't mind taping

**📝 Step-by-Step Instructions:**
1. Put the catching box on the floor at the bottom. You aim the whole run at that box.
2. Work upward. She chooses each next position, you tape it. Narrate as you go: *I'm taping this one steeper, let's see.*
3. Send one bean down the whole run after each piece you add.
4. It will fail somewhere. Look at where the bean stopped, change that one joint and nothing else, then test again.
5. Once beans run the whole way, try pasta, then the ball. Each weight runs the course in its own way, and she will spot that before you say anything.
6. Leave it taped up. You take it down tomorrow as part of the demolition.

> 💡 **Tip:** Hand her the words. *This end is higher than that end* teaches more here than *is this end higher or lower?*, and it keeps her building.

### 🌳 Get Outside: Stick Bridges

Find a puddle, a gutter or a gap between two stones and build a bridge across
it with sticks. Test each one with a pebble. Most of them fall in. Ask her
what a longer stick does, then what two sticks laid side by side do. The
gloves come home wet.

> 👶 **Infant Integration:** Kreston is 6 months and tracks moving objects
> across his whole field of view now. Sit him facing the ball run and drop a
> bean down it while he watches. Do it four or five times. He loses the bean at
> the top and finds it again at the bottom, which is early object permanence
> work, and it costs you nothing but pointing him in the right direction.

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

### 🎨 Second Main Event: The Bridge That Holds Teddy

The bridge goes between two chairs and has to hold a teddy while the gap keeps
growing. Cardboard gets strong once she changes its shape.

**🧰 Complete Materials List:**
- 2 dining chairs, set a hand's width apart to start
- Flattened cardboard, several pieces
- Lolly sticks and cardboard tubes
- 1 roll masking tape
- 1 teddy or doll
- A tin of beans as the heavy test weight

**📝 Step-by-Step Instructions:**
1. Set the chairs close together. Lay one flat piece of card between them and stand teddy on it. It holds.
2. Move the chairs a hand's width apart and try again. Keep going until it sags.
3. Ask *what could we change?* and wait. Odds are she says a bigger piece of card. Try it, and watch it sag again.
4. Show her one trick and then step back: fold the card into a concertina, or roll it into tubes and lay them side by side. Let her work out which way up it goes.
5. Test with teddy, then the tin of beans, then both at once.
6. Push the chairs as far apart as the bridge will survive and mark the floor with tape.
7. Take a photo of the winning bridge with teddy standing on it.

> 💡 **Tip:** When she gets stuck, count to five in your head before you help. Most of the time she starts again on her own inside those five seconds, and the fix she finds is worth more than yours.

### 🎯 Afternoon Alternatives
- Bridge two sofa cushions with one piece of card and stop there.
- Build a bridge for toy cars instead of teddy.
- Fold paper into concertinas and stand books on them.
- Lay a plank between two low stools outside and walk across it.

### 🌳 Out Again: **Pick one**
- A long walk with the pram, the whole loop, no destination
- Feed the ducks and count the bridges you cross
- The park, for the climbing frame she can look at as a structure now
- A drive to somewhere with a real bridge, and stand under it
- The playground with the big slide, which is a ramp she can ride

> 👶 **Kreston's Afternoon:** Lay him under the finished bridge and let him
> look up through it. At 6 months he is rocking on hands and knees without
> moving forward yet, so set the teddy a little past his reach on the far side
> of the bridge. He rocks toward something he wants.

---

> 🧠 **A Little Parenting Insight**
> **Narrating beats quizzing, every time.**
> You will want to turn the morning into a quiz (*what colour is that? how
> many? is it big or small?*), because her answers feel like proof that she
> learned something. You end up with a three-year-old performing for an adult.
> Give her the running commentary instead and she gets new vocabulary in a real
> context, sentence structures she has not built yet, and no pressure to be
> right. Parents who narrate hand their children more words to hear, and a
> wider range of them. Save your questions for the ones you cannot answer
> yourself, like *what could we change?* over a bridge that keeps sagging.

> ⚠️ **Safety:** Dried beans and pasta are a choking hazard and they will end
> up on the floor. Sweep before you put Kreston down, and keep the catching box
> off the floor when he is loose in the room. Scissors stay with you.

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

## 🌟 Day 4: Demolition Day 🌟
**📅 Monday, January 4, 2027**
**Theme:** Shape Engineers & Building

### 🌙 Prep Tonight
> Mix the salt dough for tomorrow: 2 cups flour, 1 cup salt, 1 cup water. Bag it and leave it in the fridge, and put out a clean sock and a ball of string for today.

### ⏰ At-a-Glance Schedule
- **8:00–9:00 AM** — 🌅 Opening Activity: Cup Pyramids
- **9:00–9:15 AM** — Morning Meeting (15 min)
- **9:15–10:15 AM** — 🎨 The Main Event: The Knock-Down Experiment (60 min)
- **10:15–10:30 AM** — Snack (15 min)
- **10:30–11:15 AM** — 🌳 Get Outside: Stone Stacking (45 min)
- **11:15–11:45 AM** — Free Play (30 min)
- **11:45 AM–12:00 PM** — 🤸 Wiggle Time (15 min)
- **12:00–12:30 PM** — Lunch
- **12:30–1:00 PM** — 📚 Book Time
- **1:00–2:30 PM** — 🎨 Second Main Event: The Wrecking Ball (90 min)
- **2:30–3:30 PM** — 🌳 Out Again: **Pick one** (60 min)
- **3:30–4:00 PM** — Snack & Free Play
- **4:00–4:15 PM** — 🧹 Her Job: The Forks
- **4:15–5:00 PM** — Quiet Play: Rebuilding Alone
- **5:00–5:45 PM** — 🛁 Wind-Down: Bath and the Quiet Corner
- **6:00 PM** — **Dinner**

### 🌅 Opening Activity: Cup Pyramids

**🧰 You need:** paper cups, 20 or so · a flat bit of floor.

1. Build a pyramid together: six cups along the bottom, then five, then four.
2. Take one cup out of the bottom row and see what happens. Then put it back and take one from the top instead.
3. Ask which cup is doing the most work. Take her answer as it comes and tell her you will test it for real at 9:15.

### 🎨 The Main Event: The Knock-Down Experiment

**🧰 Complete Materials List:**
- Boxes, cups and tubes from the build box
- 1 roll masking tape
- Paper and a marker for recording
- The ball run from yesterday, still taped to the wall

**📝 Step-by-Step Instructions:**
1. Build three towers side by side, all the same height. Tower one has a wide base. Tower two is a straight stack. Tower three is widest at the top on purpose.
2. Give each one a number and write the numbers on paper.
3. Push the floor near tower one with a flat hand. Count how many pushes before it goes. Write the number down. Repeat for two and three.
4. Ask her which fell first and which held longest, and write her explanation word for word next to the numbers.
5. Rebuild the winner and let her push it over herself, as hard as she likes.
6. Now take down the ball run together and add its tubes to the pile.

> 💡 **Tip:** Do not save the towers. You need them to fall to get the count, and the moment you ask her to be careful with one, she stops testing it.

### 🌳 Get Outside: Stone Stacking

Find flat stones and stack them as high as they will go on a wall, a step or a
tree stump. She learned the wide-base rule indoors and will use it out here
before you mention it. Leave the stack standing for the next person and walk
past it tomorrow to see whether it survived.

> 👶 **Infant Integration:** Kreston is 6 months. Build him a two-cup tower
> within arm's reach and let him swipe it down. He will do it again and again
> without tiring of it, the same experiment Azlyn is running with three
> towers and a sheet of paper. Keep his cups separate from hers so nothing
> chewed goes back in the build box.

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

### 🎨 Second Main Event: The Wrecking Ball

This morning she knocked the towers over with her own hands. This afternoon
she pulls a weight back, lets go, and the swing does the work for her.

**🧰 Complete Materials List:**
- 1 clean sock
- Dried beans or rice to fill it
- 1 ball of string, about two metres
- A door frame, a broom handle across two chairs, or a curtain rail bracket
- All the boxes and cups
- Masking tape

**📝 Step-by-Step Instructions:**
1. Fill the sock a third full, knot it, and tie the string tight around the knot.
2. Hang the free end from the door frame so the sock swings a hand's width above the floor.
3. Build a tower right under the swing. Pull the sock back and let go. Do not throw it.
4. Rebuild in the same spot and try releasing from lower down. Ask her which knocked more over.
5. Move the tower further away and let her work out where the swing stops reaching.
6. Let her build a tower she thinks can survive it. Test it. Let her be right or wrong without commentary.
7. Finish by building one tower together that you both agree is too good to knock down, then knock it down anyway.

> 💡 **Tip:** She will want to swing the sock by hand. Let her, once, then go back to releasing it. The height she starts it from decides how much falls over, and she sees that when she lets go instead of pushing.

### 🎯 Afternoon Alternatives
- Roll a ball at the towers instead of swinging anything.
- Knock towers over with a cardboard tube used as a bat.
- Build and flatten with feet only, no hands.
- Stack cushions and jump into them.

### 🌳 Out Again: **Pick one**
- The riverside path, to look at what holds the bank up
- The woods loop, hunting for fallen trees and what pulled them down
- The neighbourhood park
- The duck pond, and the bridge over it
- A green space with a ball, for anyone who has had enough of indoors

> 👶 **Kreston's Afternoon:** Keep him well clear of the swinging sock. Put him
> in the far corner with the flattened box he liked on Friday. Once the
> wrecking is over, lie him on his back and swing a soft toy above him at a
> slow, steady pace. At 6 months he tracks a pendulum in one smooth sweep and
> stays with it longer than you would guess.

---

> 🧠 **A Little Parenting Insight**
> **Knocking it down *is* the science.**
> You watch her flatten something she spent forty minutes making, and it looks
> like forty minutes thrown out. She made a prediction while she built it, and
> knocking it down is how she collects the answer, so the demolition carries as
> much of the learning as the stacking did. Children work out the physical
> rules of the world by breaking things and watching how they break, which is
> why the same tower goes up and down eleven times. Say *don't, you worked so
> hard on that* and you save the cardboard while ending the experiment. Take a
> photo if you want the tower kept, then let her flatten it.

> ⚠️ **Safety:** Tie the string to something that cannot come down on top of
> her: a door frame, not a curtain pole or a shelf. Take the sock down and put
> the string away the moment you finish. A hanging loop of string is a
> strangulation risk, so do not leave it up overnight or within reach of
> either child.

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

<a id="day-5"></a>

## 🌟 Day 5: Bricks and Mortar 🌟
**📅 Tuesday, January 5, 2027**
**Theme:** Shape Engineers & Building

### 🌙 Prep Tonight
> The salt dough is mixed and waiting in the fridge. Take it out before bed so it comes to room temperature, and clear a low oven shelf.

### ⏰ At-a-Glance Schedule
- **8:00–9:00 AM** — 🌅 Opening Activity: Rolling the Bricks
- **9:00–9:15 AM** — Morning Meeting (15 min)
- **9:15–10:15 AM** — 🎨 The Main Event: Salt Dough Bricks (60 min)
- **10:15–10:30 AM** — Snack (15 min)
- **10:30–11:15 AM** — 🌳 Get Outside: The Cold Walk (45 min)
- **11:15–11:45 AM** — Free Play (30 min)
- **11:45 AM–12:00 PM** — 🤸 Wiggle Time (15 min)
- **12:00–12:30 PM** — Lunch
- **12:30–1:00 PM** — 📚 Book Time
- **1:00–2:30 PM** — 🎨 Second Main Event: Building the Brick Wall (90 min)
- **2:30–3:30 PM** — 🌳 Out Again: **Swimming** (60 min)
- **3:30–4:00 PM** — Snack & Free Play
- **4:00–4:15 PM** — 🧹 Her Job: The Forks
- **4:15–5:00 PM** — Quiet Play: The Leftover Dough
- **5:00–5:45 PM** — 🛁 Wind-Down: Bath and Two Books
- **6:00 PM** — **Dinner**

### 🌅 Opening Activity: Rolling the Bricks

**🧰 You need:** the salt dough · a rolling pin · a table knife · a floured board.

1. Give her a fist-sized lump and let her squash it for a few minutes before anything useful happens.
2. Let her roll it out to about a finger's thickness and leave the uneven patches where they are.
3. Cut rough rectangles with the table knife. They do not need to match. Aim for thirty and stop when she stops.

### 🎨 The Main Event: Salt Dough Bricks

**🧰 Complete Materials List:**
- The salt dough, 2 cups flour to 1 cup salt to 1 cup water
- 1 rolling pin, 1 table knife
- 1 baking tray and baking paper
- Extra flour for the board
- 1 pencil for scoring

**📝 Step-by-Step Instructions:**
1. Lay the cut bricks on the lined tray without touching each other.
2. Let her press a mark into each one with her thumb, or scratch a line with the pencil. She owns the ones she marks.
3. Set them in the oven at its lowest setting. They need two to three hours, so put them in now and take them out around lunch.
4. While they bake, knead the leftover dough together. Give her a big lump, take one yourself, and sit with it. Ten minutes of quiet kneading does more for a wound-up morning than any instruction.
5. Make three or four larger pieces (a doorstep, an arch, a chimney) and add them to a second tray.
6. When they come out, spread them on a cold tray and leave them until 1:00, because warm bricks crumble.

> 💡 **Tip:** They will not all survive. Bake ten more than you need and hand the broken ones back to her as rubble, which she will play with more than the whole ones.

### 🌳 Get Outside: The Cold Walk

Keep it short and brisk today, because the oven is on. Go past yesterday's
stone stack first and see whether it survived. Look for frost on car
roofs and in the shade at the bottom of walls. Let her breathe out and watch
it. Find one brick wall and put her hand flat on it so she can feel how cold
stone gets and see how many bricks it took.

> 👶 **Infant Integration:** **Kreston turns 7 months today.** He has started
> reaching across his own body for something on the far side, which is new, so
> seal a spare lump of dough in a freezer bag and set it a little past his
> midline for him to stretch towards. Keep it sealed, because salt dough is
> far too salty to be eaten. Say happy seven months out loud, because
> nobody else will.

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

### 🎨 Second Main Event: Building the Brick Wall

The wall goes up on a tray, one row at a time. She lays every brick herself,
and the thirty she cut are the whole supply.

**🧰 Complete Materials List:**
- The cooled salt dough bricks, thirty or so
- A flat tray or baking sheet as the building site
- 1 small bowl of flour and water paste
- 1 teaspoon for the paste
- A ruler or a straight piece of card
- The teddy from Sunday

**📝 Step-by-Step Instructions:**
1. Lay a row of bricks in a straight line along the tray. Push them tight together.
2. Build a second row straight on top with every joint matching. Push it and watch the whole wall hinge apart.
3. Rebuild the second row offset, each brick bridging the gap below it. Push again. This one holds, and she will feel the difference in her hand.
4. Keep going up. Use the paste between rows like mortar if the wall gets tall enough to need it.
5. Leave a gap for a door and lay one long brick across the top of it. That is an arch, and it is holding up everything above it.
6. Build a second wall at a right angle to the first so they lean on each other.
7. Stand teddy inside. Photograph the wall next to the tower from Day 1.

> 💡 **Tip:** Do not explain the offset in step three before she has seen the matched-joint wall fall apart. She needs the collapse first for the fix to mean anything.

### 🎯 Afternoon Alternatives
- Bricks only, no paste, and knock it down when it bores her.
- Press leaves and buttons into the leftover dough instead.
- Paint the dried bricks and build tomorrow.
- Sort the bricks by size into rows and stop there.

### 🌳 Out Again: **Swimming**
- **Swimming**, the weekly one and the first of fifty-two
- Soft play if the pool is shut for the holidays
- The library, a day early
- A warm café and a window, if nobody has the energy
- The indoor play centre

> 👶 **Kreston's Afternoon:** He has swimming today too if he is coming. At 7
> months he can sit supported in the water and splash with real intent. Keep it
> to twenty minutes and get him out before he is cold. Hold him chest to chest
> facing you for the first few minutes so the noise arrives with your face
> already in front of him.

---

> 🧠 **A Little Parenting Insight**
> **Why kneading dough calms a wound-up kid down.**
> Push, squeeze and pull something that resists you, and you are doing what
> occupational therapists call heavy work, one of the steadiest ways to settle
> a small body. The pressure through her hands and joints feeds her
> proprioceptive sense, the one that tracks where her body is, and a kid who
> has lost track of it is the one climbing the back of the sofa. Dough costs
> pennies and pushes back for as long as she keeps working it. Ten minutes of
> it on a morning that has gone loud will settle her further than a talk about
> calming down.

> ⚠️ **Safety:** The oven is on for three hours today, so say it out loud
> when she comes near, and keep Kreston out of the kitchen while the trays
> move. Salt dough is dangerous if eaten, and seriously so for a 7-month-old
> and for any dog in the house. Count the bricks in and out and store them
> high.

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

<a id="day-6"></a>

## 🌟 Day 6: Triangles Hold 🌟
**📅 Wednesday, January 6, 2027**
**Theme:** Shape Engineers & Building

### 🌙 Prep Tonight
> Put the lolly sticks, the string and the masking tape on the table, and stand two dining chairs about two metres apart in the living room.

### ⏰ At-a-Glance Schedule
- **8:00–9:00 AM** — 🌅 Opening Activity: Lolly Stick Shapes
- **9:00–9:15 AM** — Morning Meeting (15 min)
- **9:15–10:15 AM** — 🎨 The Main Event: The Triangle Test (60 min)
- **10:15–10:30 AM** — Snack (15 min)
- **10:30–11:15 AM** — 🌳 Get Outside: The Stick Shelter (45 min)
- **11:15–11:45 AM** — Free Play (30 min)
- **11:45 AM–12:00 PM** — 🤸 Wiggle Time (15 min)
- **12:00–12:30 PM** — Lunch
- **12:30–1:00 PM** — 📚 Book Time
- **1:00–2:30 PM** — 🎨 Second Main Event: The Zip Line (90 min)
- **2:30–3:30 PM** — 🌳 Out Again: **The Library** (60 min)
- **3:30–4:00 PM** — Snack & Free Play
- **4:00–4:15 PM** — 🧹 Her Job: The Forks
- **4:15–5:00 PM** — Quiet Play: The Sticker Page
- **5:00–5:45 PM** — 🛁 Wind-Down: The New Library Book
- **6:00 PM** — **Dinner**

### 🌅 Opening Activity: Lolly Stick Shapes

**🧰 You need:** lolly sticks · masking tape cut into short tabs.

1. Tape four sticks into a square. Let her do the taping, however it comes out, with your thumb holding the corners.
2. Tape three sticks into a triangle beside it.
3. Press one finger on the top corner of each. The square folds flat and the triangle holds. Say nothing else about it and leave both on the table until 9:15.

### 🎨 The Main Event: The Triangle Test

**🧰 Complete Materials List:**
- 30–40 lolly sticks
- 1 roll masking tape
- The square and triangle from this morning
- Cardboard tubes, 4 or 5
- A small toy as the test load

**📝 Step-by-Step Instructions:**
1. Hand her the folded square and ask her to make it stop folding. Then wait, and hold the silence a good while longer than feels comfortable.
2. If she gets stuck, put one loose stick on the table beside it and say nothing more. Most children lay it across the diagonal within a minute.
3. Once the square has a diagonal, push it together again. It holds now, because the diagonal turned it into two triangles.
4. Build a tall tower out of taped triangles. Build a second out of taped squares. Race them upward.
5. Load both with the toy and push both over. Write down which survived.
6. Go and look at the box house from Saturday and find where a triangle would fix its saggy roof. Add one.

> 💡 **Tip:** Sit on your hands today. Every joint you fix for her takes away the exact thing the activity exists to teach.

### 🌳 Get Outside: The Stick Shelter

Lean long sticks against a fence, a low branch or a wall to make a tent shape,
then let go and see whether it stands. Push the top the way she pushed the one
on the table. A triangle out here behaves the same as one indoors. Put a stone
or a leaf inside for her to shelter, and come back tomorrow to see whether the
wind took it.

> 👶 **Infant Integration:** Kreston is 7 months and starting to pull himself
> forward. Lay the taped triangle tower on its side within his reach and let
> him post his fingers through the gaps. He is learning that a shape has an
> inside. His hand goes in, meets air, comes back out, and then he tries the
> next gap along.

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

### 🎨 Second Main Event: The Zip Line

She has spent the week building things that push down. A zip line pulls, the
opposite force and a new idea for her.

**🧰 Complete Materials List:**
- 1 ball of string, 3 metres
- 2 dining chairs, or a door handle and a chair
- 1 paper cup
- 1 cardboard tube cut into a short ring
- Masking tape and scissors
- Small toys, a spoon, a dried bean (cargo of different weights)

**📝 Step-by-Step Instructions:**
1. Tie the string tight between the chair backs, one end higher than the other. She decides which end is higher.
2. Tape the paper cup to the cardboard ring so the cup hangs underneath, then thread the string through the ring before you tie the second end.
3. Load the cup with a small toy at the high end and let go.
4. Once it stops halfway, ask what would make it keep going. Raise the high end and try again. Let her be the one who lifts it.
5. Race two different weights down the same line, and have her call the winner out loud before she lets go.
6. Slacken the string right off and try again so she sees a sagging line stop the cup dead.
7. Send one thing down for each person in the house and let her decide who gets what.

> 💡 **Tip:** Ask her to predict before each run. Let her be wrong out loud, then watch. She keeps more from that than from a right answer she kept in her head.

### 🎯 Afternoon Alternatives
- One string, one cup, no ring. It slides fine.
- Post things down the bannister instead.
- Make a pulley by looping the string over a door handle and hauling a bag up.
- Tape a string across the bath and float things along it.

### 🌳 Out Again: **Pick one**
- **The library** (fortnightly, and the first visit of the year)
- Soft play if the library is packed with the holiday crowd
- The shops, and let her carry one thing
- The park on the way home
- A café and the new book, read cover to cover once

> 👶 **Kreston's Afternoon:** Take him to the library too and sit him in the
> board-book corner. At 7 months he will chew the books, which is what board
> books are built for. Pick one with high-contrast pages. Let Azlyn choose his
> and carry it to the desk herself.

---

> 🧠 **A Little Parenting Insight**
> **The pause before you help.**
> Azlyn wrestling with a joint that will not hold looks like a child who needs
> you. Most of the time she needs four more seconds. Adults step in fast, often
> before the frustration has arrived, and every early rescue teaches the same
> background lesson: hard things get solved by someone else. Count to five
> before you move. If she is still stuck, give her the smallest nudge you can,
> the loose stick set on the table beside her while your hands stay off the
> tape. You are protecting the willingness she will need next year to stay with
> a hard thing.

> ⚠️ **Safety:** Take the zip line down and put the string away the second you
> finish. String at toddler height is a strangulation risk, and a serious one.
> Loose lolly sticks stay off the floor while Kreston is down. They splinter,
> and they fit down his throat. He gets a taped structure too big for his mouth
> and nothing looser than that, and it comes back to you the moment you stop
> watching. The scissors stay with you.

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

<a id="day-7"></a>

## 🌟 Day 7: The Fort 🌟
**📅 Thursday, January 7, 2027**
**Theme:** Shape Engineers & Building

### 🌙 Prep Tonight
> Get every blanket, sheet and spare pillow out of the cupboard and pile them on the sofa. Check the torch batteries.

### ⏰ At-a-Glance Schedule
- **8:00–9:00 AM** — 🌅 Opening Activity: Choosing the Fort Spot
- **9:00–9:15 AM** — Morning Meeting (15 min)
- **9:15–10:15 AM** — 🎨 The Main Event: The Blanket Fort (60 min)
- **10:15–10:30 AM** — Snack (15 min)
- **10:30–11:15 AM** — 🌳 Get Outside: The Outdoor Den (45 min)
- **11:15–11:45 AM** — Free Play (30 min)
- **11:45 AM–12:00 PM** — 🤸 Wiggle Time (15 min)
- **12:00–12:30 PM** — Lunch
- **12:30–1:00 PM** — 📚 Book Time
- **1:00–2:30 PM** — 🎨 Second Main Event: Living in the Fort (90 min)
- **2:30–3:30 PM** — 🌳 Out Again: **Pick one** (60 min)
- **3:30–4:00 PM** — Snack & Free Play
- **4:00–4:15 PM** — 🧹 Her Job: The Forks
- **4:15–5:00 PM** — Quiet Play: Alone in the Fort
- **5:00–5:45 PM** — 🛁 Wind-Down: The Week in Photos
- **6:00 PM** — **Dinner**

### 🌅 Opening Activity: Choosing the Fort Spot

**🧰 You need:** the blanket pile · masking tape · her.

1. Walk her round the downstairs and ask where the fort should go. Take the answer seriously even if it is the hallway.
2. Test it together. Can two people sit up in it, is there something to hang a blanket from, will the door still open.
3. Mark the corners on the floor with tape. That is the footprint, and the fort you build at 9:15 stays inside it.

### 🎨 The Main Event: The Blanket Fort

**🧰 Complete Materials List:**
- 3–4 blankets or sheets
- 4 dining chairs
- Sofa cushions
- Clothes pegs or bulldog clips
- Heavy books to weight the edges
- The salt dough bricks from Tuesday

**📝 Step-by-Step Instructions:**
1. Set the chairs on the tape marks with their backs outward. That is the frame.
2. Drape the first sheet across the top and clip it to the chair backs. Clips hold where tucked edges pull loose.
3. Ask her where the door goes. Peg that edge so it can be lifted and dropped.
4. Send her inside with the cushions to build the floor. Leave her to it for a full ten minutes.
5. Once the roof sags, remind her what fixed the sagging bridge on Sunday and let her work out the rest.
6. Weight every outside edge with books and line the doorway with the salt dough bricks, so she walks past Tuesday's work on her way in.

> 💡 **Tip:** Build it to survive her crawling through it. A fort that collapses every four minutes becomes a chore for both of you.

### 🌳 Get Outside: The Outdoor Den

Do the same thing outside with whatever is there, whether that is a bench and a
blanket, sticks against a fence, or a sheet over the washing line. Check
Wednesday's stick shelter on the way past and see whether the wind took it. Get
her to sit inside the outdoor den for a minute in the cold, then come in to
the warm one.

> 👶 **Infant Integration:** Kreston is 7 months and this is his best day of the
> week. Lay him on his back inside the fort and let him look up at the light
> through a sheet. Sit him against a cushion by the doorway so he can watch
> Azlyn come and go, which at this age is a game in itself. Keep the doorway
> open, keep cushions clear of his face, and stay in there with him.

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

### 🎨 Second Main Event: Living in the Fort

The whole afternoon happens inside the fort. She eats in there, reads in
there, draws in there, and it stays up until bedtime.

**🧰 Complete Materials List:**
- The fort
- 1 torch
- A picnic blanket or towel for the floor
- Snack and a drink in a lidded cup
- 4–5 books
- Paper and crayons
- Your phone, for the week's photos

**📝 Step-by-Step Instructions:**
1. Move in. Floor covering first, then the torch, then the books.
2. Eat the afternoon snack inside. Food in a fort is most of the appeal.
3. Turn the big light off and do shadow shapes on the roof (triangle, square, house).
4. Read two books in there by torchlight, and let her hold the torch even though it will point at the ceiling the whole time.
5. Look back through the week's photos together: the tower, the box house, the bridge with teddy on it, the brick wall. Ask which one she would build again.
6. Let her draw the fort from the inside while she is inside it.
7. Leave it standing overnight. Tomorrow you turn it into something else.

> 💡 **Tip:** Go in with her, then stop directing. The hour has nothing to achieve, and she spends it in a small space she chose the shape of.

### 🎯 Afternoon Alternatives
- One sheet over the dining table. Four minutes, same result.
- Read in the fort and skip everything else.
- Torch and shadow puppets on the wall instead.
- Take the fort down and rebuild it somewhere else entirely.

### 🌳 Out Again: **Pick one**
- The grocery shop, and let her choose one thing for the fort picnic
- The park, for the tunnel and the climbing frame
- A friend's house, if the fort can wait an hour
- Feed the ducks and look at where they shelter
- The hardware shop, to look at real roofs and joists

> 👶 **Kreston's Afternoon:** Take him into the shop in the carrier facing out.
> At 7 months the noise, lights and strangers of a supermarket are work for him,
> so twenty minutes is plenty. Back home, put him in the fort doorway with one
> board book while Azlyn reads inside. He will hold it upside down and be
> pleased with it.

---

> 🧠 **A Little Parenting Insight**
> **Why small enclosed spaces are so appealing right now.**
> Azlyn goes under the table, into the cupboard, behind the sofa. She hides in
> them, and she also uses them to shrink the world. A small space cuts how much
> arrives at once, and she cannot filter noise and light and movement yet, so
> walls close enough to touch give her relief. The fort is also the first
> territory she controls: she picked the shape, the door and who comes in. If
> she wants to be in there on her own, let her. Leave it up for a few days.

> ⚠️ **Safety:** Nothing heavy on top of the fort and nothing that can fall
> inward. Books go on the outside edges, never on the roof. No cords, no fairy
> lights, no string left from yesterday. Never leave Kreston in there on his own,
> and keep pillows and loose blankets away from his face.

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
<a id="week-2"></a>

## Week 2: Nursery Rhymes & Rhythm 🎶
**📅 January 8 – January 14, 2027**

---

### 🎯 What This Week Is About

Every rhyme she knows is a machine for keeping time, and this week she takes
them apart. She fills five shakers and lines them up quietest to loudest, drops
an egg off a wall she built herself, counts five ducks down to none and back
again, and hits an upturned saucepan once for every piece of a word. The rhymes
stay the same all week. Her job with them changes each morning.

No holiday lands in this stretch, so the week runs on its own thread and the
gear piles up as it goes. Five sealed shakers on Friday, a kitchen drum kit on
Monday, a hat and a flag on Thursday, and by the last afternoon the whole house
is marching a route Azlyn drew on a sheet of paper on the floor. Three
afternoons end with your phone recording her singing, one of them a song she
writes about Kreston. Back those up before Thursday.

---

### 🧒 What Azlyn Will Learn

- **The beat inside a word.** AZ-LYN is two hits, SPA-GHET-TI is four, and she hears the pieces long before she can name them.
- **Loud and quiet on purpose.** Scarf up is loud, scarf down is quiet, and she is the one holding the scarf.
- **Counting down, and starting again from one.** Five ducks to none, ten in the bed to one, five buns off a tray.
- **Calling it before it happens.** She says cracked or not cracked out loud, then opens her hand over the tray and finds out.
- **Patterns she can hear.** Spoon, cup, spoon, cup, and the gap where you took one out of the middle.

---

### 👩 What You'll Learn

- Why she only has one volume, and it is loud
- Why the rhymes she loves are all a bit gruesome
- Why she wants the same rhyme forty times
- Rhythm and reading are more connected than they look
- Why a wind-down object works better than a wind-down instruction
- Counting words come long before counting means anything
- Why moving together matters more than moving

---

### 🛒 This Week's Shopping List

> *Everything both main events call for, all seven days. Check what you've
> already got before Friday.*

- [ ] Eggs, at least 4, boiled Friday night and left to go cold
- [ ] Dried rice, 1 cup
- [ ] Dried lentils, 1 cup
- [ ] Dried pasta shapes, 1 cup
- [ ] Sugar, 2 tablespoons
- [ ] Dry cereal, a handful
- [ ] Plain flour, for the paste
- [ ] Oats or a bag of frozen peas, for the ducks
- [ ] Milk and a cinnamon stick, for the bedtime drink
- [ ] Five bread rolls, if you want real buns in the bun shop
- [ ] 5 small plastic bottles or yoghurt pots with lids
- [ ] Empty cereal boxes, 4 (start saving now)
- [ ] A large biscuit tin, or any empty tin
- [ ] A plastic tub with a lid
- [ ] A shoebox
- [ ] Paper cups, one sleeve
- [ ] Cardboard tubes, 2
- [ ] A saucepan
- [ ] A tray and a baking tray
- [ ] An egg cup
- [ ] A laundry basket
- [ ] Parcel tape
- [ ] Masking tape, 2 rolls
- [ ] Permanent markers, 2
- [ ] Crayons
- [ ] Thick paper or card, 6 sheets
- [ ] Plain paper, a stack, plus the biggest single sheet in the house
- [ ] A glue stick, or flour and water paste in a saucer
- [ ] Scissors (adult only)
- [ ] A metal skewer, or a pencil sharpened to a point (adult only)
- [ ] A pencil and a tape measure
- [ ] An old magazine to push the skewer down onto
- [ ] Spoons: 2 wooden, 1 metal, 1 teaspoon, and 8 of any kind for the pattern row
- [ ] Socks, 8
- [ ] A scarf for the conductor
- [ ] A blue tea towel or a second scarf for the pond
- [ ] A tea towel to stuff inside a drum
- [ ] An old towel to line the tray
- [ ] A pillowcase or a tote bag
- [ ] A blanket, and Kreston's own blanket
- [ ] Soft toys, 10
- [ ] Sofa cushions, 4–6, and 2 pillows
- [ ] A spare duvet, or a mattress on the floor
- [ ] Books or small boxes, 6–8, to build Humpty's wall
- [ ] Clothes pegs, 5
- [ ] A torch with fresh batteries, and spares
- [ ] A watch for the rhyme bag
- [ ] A hat each
- [ ] Your phone, with room on it to record

### 💛 A Note from Joseph

> *(Draft. Rewrite this. I can guess the shape of it, not the feeling.)*
>
> Brooklyn, this is the loud week and I am sorry for it. Seven days of
> saucepans, and a three-year-old with a scarf in her fist deciding when you
> get louder. Tuesday afternoon is the one I would keep. She makes up a verse
> about her brother and you record it in one take, and I want that sent to me
> the day it happens rather than at the end of the week. Take the whisper verse
> on Friday for yourself as much as for her. By then you will have been holding
> Kreston since six in the morning.

---

> 👶 **Kreston is 7 months this week**. He reaches across his own body for
> something on the far side, moves a toy from one hand to the other to get a
> better look at it, and babbles in long strings with a beat running through
> them.

## 🌟 Day 8: Shakers and Spoons 🌟
**📅 Friday, January 8, 2027**
**Theme:** Nursery Rhymes & Rhythm

### 🌙 Prep Tonight
> Stand five empty pots and their lids by the dry-goods cupboard, and put the parcel tape and a marker beside them.

### ⏰ At-a-Glance Schedule
- **8:00–9:00 AM** — 🌅 Opening Activity: Listening to the Cupboard
- **9:00–9:15 AM** — Morning Meeting (15 min)
- **9:15–10:15 AM** — 🎨 The Main Event: The Shaker Set (60 min)
- **10:15–10:30 AM** — Snack (15 min)
- **10:30–11:15 AM** — 🌳 Get Outside: Tapping the Railings (45 min)
- **11:15–11:45 AM** — Free Play (30 min)
- **11:45 AM–12:00 PM** — 🤸 Wiggle Time (15 min)
- **12:00–12:30 PM** — Lunch
- **12:30–1:00 PM** — 📚 Book Time
- **1:00–2:30 PM** — 🎨 Second Main Event: Loud Song, Quiet Song (90 min)
- **2:30–3:30 PM** — 🌳 Out Again: **Pick one** (60 min)
- **3:30–4:00 PM** — Snack & Free Play
- **4:00–4:15 PM** — 🧹 Her Job: The Forks
- **4:15–5:00 PM** — Quiet Play: The Quietest Shaker
- **5:00–5:45 PM** — 🛁 Wind-Down: Bath and the Rhyme Book
- **6:00 PM** — **Dinner**

### 🌅 Opening Activity: Listening to the Cupboard

**🧰 You need:** the dry-goods cupboard, open · 1 metal spoon · 5 empty pots with lids.

1. Sit her in front of the open cupboard and let her tap the packets with the back of the spoon. Rice, pasta, lentils, cereal, sugar. Each answers in a different voice.
2. Shut your eyes and let her tap one for you to name. Get one wrong on purpose.
3. Line up the five pots and let her decide which packet goes in which. You label them at 9:15.

### 🎨 The Main Event: The Shaker Set

**🧰 Complete Materials List:**
- 5 small plastic bottles or yoghurt pots with lids
- Dried rice, 1 cup
- Dried lentils, 1 cup
- Dried pasta shapes, 1 cup
- Sugar, 2 tablespoons
- Dry cereal, a handful
- Parcel tape
- 1 permanent marker
- 1 sheet of paper rolled into a funnel

**📝 Step-by-Step Instructions:**
1. Roll the paper into a cone, tape the seam and stand it in the first pot. She pours, you hold. Some goes on the table and that is the price.
2. Fill each pot a third full. A pot packed to the lid makes almost no sound, so let her overfill one and hear it.
3. Tape every lid all the way round twice and press the seams flat. Write the contents on the side of each pot in marker.
4. Shake them one at a time with her eyes shut. Ask her to point at the loudest before she opens them.
5. Stand all five along the table, quietest to loudest. Move them as often as she wants.
6. Number the bases one to five once she settles. She comes back to this line at 4:15.

> 💡 **Tip:** The sugar shaker sounds like rain and she will lose interest inside a minute. Keep it. It is the quiet end of the line, and the only instrument in this afternoon's whisper verse.

### 🌳 Get Outside: Tapping the Railings

Take a wooden spoon out with you and tap what you pass. Railings ring and hold
the note, fence posts thud and stop dead, a wheelie bin booms, a brick wall
gives back nothing. January metal is cold enough that she will want gloves on
and then off again to feel it. Let her find the loudest thing on the street and
stay at it.

> 👶 **Infant Integration:** Kreston is 7 months and reaching across his own
> body for things on the far side. Sit him back from the table with a wooden
> spoon and an upturned saucepan, and put the spoon down on his far side so he
> has to cross over for it. Keep the small pots away from him. Shake one by his
> left ear, then his right, and watch him turn toward a sound he cannot see.

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

### 🎨 Second Main Event: Loud Song, Quiet Song

The shakers have their order. This afternoon Azlyn plays one rhyme four ways
and decides which way comes next.

**🧰 Complete Materials List:**
- The five sealed shakers
- 1 saucepan and 1 wooden spoon
- 1 cardboard tube for a megaphone
- 1 scarf as the conductor's signal
- Your phone, to record the last run

**📝 Step-by-Step Instructions:**
1. Sit everyone in a ring, shakers in the middle, saucepan by your knee, scarf in Azlyn's hand.
2. Pick a rhyme the whole house knows. Twinkle, Twinkle or The Wheels on the Bus. Sing it through once with a shaker each.
3. Loud version. Saucepan on the beat, cardboard tube as a megaphone, pasta and lentils flat out. One verse, and one verse alone.
4. Quiet version. Sugar shaker on its own, everyone whispering, heads close together. She drops her voice to match yours.
5. Fast, then slow. Slow is the hard one, and one verse at half speed lasts longer than either of you expects.
6. Hand her the scarf. Scarf up is loud, scarf down is quiet, and she runs the band. Follow her even when she flips it every two words.
7. Record the last run. Play it back once, then again with everyone singing on top of it.

> 💡 **Tip:** The whisper verse works better if you get smaller. Sit on the floor, lean in, let your voice go thin.

### 🎯 Afternoon Alternatives
- Two shakers and one rhyme, sat on the sofa.
- March round the kitchen table with the saucepan and nothing else.
- Sort the shakers by what is inside instead of how loud they are.
- Sing the same rhyme in a squeaky voice, a growly voice and a whisper.

### 🌳 Out Again: **Pick one**
- **A cafe with cake**, a window seat and a street to watch
- The neighbourhood park, for twenty minutes of running
- The bakery counter, and let her choose for tomorrow
- A friend's house, if anyone can take an afternoon of shakers
- Home, with the shakers in the bath

> 👶 **Kreston's Afternoon:** He is 7 months and babbling in long strings now,
> ba-ba-ba and da-da-da-da, a rhythm section of his own. Sit him in the ring
> with a folded blanket behind him. He answers singing sooner than talking, so
> leave a gap at the end of each verse and see whether he fills it. Keep the
> loud verse short and watch his face while it runs.

### 🌍 Around the World: Lullabies Everywhere 🌙

Every country puts its children to sleep with a song, and the songs resemble
each other more than the languages do. Wales has *Suo Gân*, sung on the same
slow fall as the Japanese *Itsuki no Komoriuta*. Swahili families sing *Lala
salama*, sleep safe. Researchers played babies lullabies from cultures they had
never heard, and the babies settled to those too.

> **✨ Try it:** Warm milk with a cinnamon stick, the bedtime drink in a great
> many houses. Give Azlyn hers in the same cup all week and sing one lullaby
> that is not in English.

> *Did you know?* Lullabies the world over share a slow, falling tune, which is
> why a song in a language she has never met still works on her.

---

> 🧠 **A Little Parenting Insight**
> **Why she only has one volume, and it is loud.**
> Azlyn can do quiet on request and hold it for about four seconds. The holding
> is the hard part, and it runs on the same slow-growing machinery as waiting
> her turn and stopping halfway down the hall. Volume control is inhibition,
> and a three-year-old has a thin supply. She gets better at it by practising
> the switch rather than by being asked for less noise. Handing her the loud
> verse first and the whisper verse second gives her both ends to feel in her
> own throat a minute apart. The scarf makes her the one calling the change,
> and running the switch builds it faster than obeying it.

> ⚠️ **Safety:** Dried rice, lentils and pasta on the floor are a choking
> hazard for a 7-month-old, and a taped lid can still come off. Tape all the way
> round each pot, check every seal before it goes down, and keep the small pots
> away from Kreston.

## 🌟 Day 9: Humpty Dumpty's Wall 🌟
**📅 Saturday, January 9, 2027**
**Theme:** Nursery Rhymes & Rhythm

### 🌙 Prep Tonight
> Boil four eggs and leave them in the fridge to go cold, and fold an old towel into a baking tray beside them.

### ⏰ At-a-Glance Schedule
- **8:00–9:00 AM** — 🌅 Opening Activity: Drawing on the Eggs
- **9:00–9:15 AM** — Morning Meeting (15 min)
- **9:15–10:15 AM** — 🎨 The Main Event: The Egg Drop Record (60 min)
- **10:15–10:30 AM** — Snack (15 min)
- **10:30–11:15 AM** — 🌳 Get Outside: The Low Wall Balance (45 min)
- **11:15–11:45 AM** — Free Play (30 min)
- **11:45 AM–12:00 PM** — 🤸 Wiggle Time (15 min)
- **12:00–12:30 PM** — Lunch
- **12:30–1:00 PM** — 📚 Book Time
- **1:00–2:30 PM** — 🎨 Second Main Event: The Mended Egg (90 min)
- **2:30–3:30 PM** — 🌳 Out Again: **Pick one** (60 min)
- **3:30–4:00 PM** — Snack & Free Play
- **4:00–4:15 PM** — 🧹 Her Job: The Forks
- **4:15–5:00 PM** — Quiet Play: The Egg Cups
- **5:00–5:45 PM** — 🛁 Wind-Down: The Quiet End of a Loud Day
- **6:00 PM** — **Dinner**

### 🌅 Opening Activity: Drawing on the Eggs

**🧰 You need:** 4 cold boiled eggs · 1 permanent marker · 1 egg cup.

1. Hand her the marker and one egg at a time. Faces, hats, whatever she wants. Marker takes a minute to dry on a shell, so hold each one by the ends.
2. Say the rhyme together while she draws. She will reach the line about nobody putting him together again and look up at you.
3. Stand the best one in the egg cup on the windowsill. Nobody touches him before 9:15.

### 🎨 The Main Event: The Egg Drop Record

**🧰 Complete Materials List:**
- 4 hard-boiled eggs, boiled last night and cooled
- 1 baking tray
- 1 old towel to line the tray
- 6–8 books or small boxes to build the wall
- 1 permanent marker
- Paper for the record
- 1 tape measure

**📝 Step-by-Step Instructions:**
1. Line the tray with the folded towel and set it on the kitchen floor. She stacks the books into a wall beside it.
2. Sit Humpty on top and say the rhyme once with him up there.
3. First drop from ankle height, straight into the tray. Measure with the tape and write the number down before she opens her hand.
4. Ask her to call it before every drop: cracked or not cracked. Write her answer beside the number. Knee height next, then hip.
5. Take the towel out for the last egg and drop it on the bare tray from ankle height. It breaks where it was safe five minutes ago.
6. Read the paper back to her. Her numbers, her guesses, and the one that caught her out.

> 💡 **Tip:** Let her hold the egg and let her be the one who opens her hand. An egg she drops herself is her experiment.

### 🌳 Get Outside: The Low Wall Balance

Find a low garden wall, a kerb edge or the flat top of a step and walk her
along it holding one hand. She sits at the far end with her legs over and says
the rhyme from up there, then jumps into your hands. January stone keeps its
frost all morning in the shade, so put your own hand flat on the top first.

> 👶 **Infant Integration:** Kreston is 7 months and has started dropping food
> over the side of the high chair and leaning out to look. Same experiment,
> cheaper equipment. Sit him where he can see the tray for three or four drops.
> Then give him an egg cup on his own tray and pick it up eight times without
> comment.

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

### 🎨 Second Main Event: The Mended Egg

Humpty's shell is still sitting on the tray. She glues him back together this
afternoon, the one thing the rhyme insists nobody could do.

**🧰 Complete Materials List:**
- The cracked shells from this morning
- 1 sheet of thick paper or card
- A glue stick, or flour and water paste in a saucer
- 1 teaspoon for the paste
- Crayons
- 1 pencil to draw the egg outline

**📝 Step-by-Step Instructions:**
1. Draw a big egg outline on the card in pencil, or let her draw it and go over her line so she can see it.
2. Tip the shell onto a sheet of paper and let her press it small with the back of a spoon. Fingers work too.
3. Mix the paste if you are using it: two tablespoons of flour, water stirred in until it runs like cream.
4. Cover a small patch of the egg at a time. Paste the whole outline at once and it dries before she has filled a third of it.
5. She presses the shell down. Let her go outside the line and leave gaps, and keep your hands off.
6. Crayon in the rest: the wall underneath, the king's horses, a face on the shell.
7. Write her title along the bottom in her own words, then put it up high to dry.

> 💡 **Tip:** The shell runs out near the top and she will notice. Crayon fills the gap, and the picture is better for one bald patch.

### 🎯 Afternoon Alternatives
- Shells into a bowl of water and stir. That is the whole activity.
- Draw Humpty on a flattened box and glue nothing.
- Paint the shells today and glue them tomorrow.
- Sort the pieces into big, medium and tiny on three saucers.

### 🌳 Out Again: **Pick one**
- **A bus ride to nowhere in particular**, upstairs at the front
- The park, for the low wall she balanced on this morning
- Soft play if the weather has gone sideways
- A walk to look at real brick walls and count the courses
- The shop for eggs, since you are four down

> 👶 **Kreston's Afternoon:** Keep him at the table and off the shells. Stick a
> long strip of masking tape to his high chair tray and let him work it loose.
> At 7 months he moves a thing from one hand to the other for a better angle.
> On the bus he wants the window, so face him out on your lap and name the
> stops.

---

> 🧠 **A Little Parenting Insight**
> **Why the rhymes she loves are all a bit gruesome.**
> Humpty smashes. The cradle falls out of the tree, baby and all. Three mice
> lose their tails to a carving knife. Azlyn asks for these on repeat and then
> sleeps fine, because a rhyme keeps a small disaster inside a shape that never
> alters. She knows the ending before it arrives, so the fall frightens her the
> way a slide frightens her, with the floor already in view. Rehearsing an
> alarming thing at a safe distance is how she works out its real size.
> Dropping the egg herself went further again: she caused the crack, she stood
> over it, and it turned out smaller than the rhyme makes it sound.

> ⚠️ **Safety:** Shell fragments have sharp edges and are exactly the size
> Kreston puts in his mouth. Keep the tray and the glued sheet up high, sweep
> the floor before he goes down, and wipe egg off a hard floor straight away
> because it is slippery.

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

## 🌟 Day 10: Five Little Ducks 🌟
**📅 Sunday, January 10, 2027**
**Theme:** Nursery Rhymes & Rhythm

### 🌙 Prep Tonight
> Flatten a cereal box for the ducks, stand the laundry basket upside down in the living room, and put a pillowcase beside it.

### ⏰ At-a-Glance Schedule

- **10:30–11:15 AM** — 🌳 Get Outside: Walking in a Duck Line (45 min)
- **11:15–11:45 AM** — Free Play (30 min)
- **11:45 AM–12:00 PM** — 🤸 Wiggle Time (15 min)
- **12:00–12:30 PM** — Lunch
- **12:30–1:00 PM** — 📚 Book Time
- **1:00–2:30 PM** — 🎨 Second Main Event: The Rhyme Bag (90 min)

- **3:30–4:00 PM** — Snack & Free Play

### 🌅 Opening Activity: Making the Five Ducks

**🧰 You need:** 1 flattened cereal box · scissors (adult only) · crayons · 5 clothes pegs.

1. Draw five duck shapes on the plain side of the box. Rough ovals with a beak on the front will do.
2. You cut, she colours. Give each one something different, and let her choose which is the mother duck.
3. Clip a peg to the bottom of each so they stand up. Line all five along the table and count them twice.

### 🎨 The Main Event: Over the Hill and Far Away

**🧰 Complete Materials List:**
- 1 cereal box, for cutting five duck shapes
- Scissors (adult only)
- Crayons
- 5 clothes pegs to stand the ducks up
- 1 upturned laundry basket or a sofa cushion
- 1 blue tea towel or scarf for the pond
- Masking tape

**📝 Step-by-Step Instructions:**
1. Turn the laundry basket upside down in the middle of the floor for the hill. Spread the tea towel in front of it for the pond and tape the corners flat.
2. Stand all five ducks on the pond. Run a strip of masking tape across the floor behind the hill to mark where far away starts.
3. Sing the first verse. On the line about only four coming back, she takes one duck over the hill and stands it on the tape.
4. Count what is left on the pond after each verse, touching each duck as its number comes. She does the quacking, all of it.
5. Keep going down to none, and sit in the quiet of an empty pond before the last verse.
6. Mother duck calls and all five come back over together. She carries them, and she will want the whole thing again, so do it again.

> 💡 **Tip:** Put the vanished ducks somewhere she can see the tops of. A duck gone for good gets checked on halfway through the next verse.

### 🌳 Get Outside: Walking in a Duck Line

Walk the block in single file. She goes first as the mother duck, everyone
behind her waddles, and at each corner the person at the back moves to the
front. Count off before you set out and count again at home to see whether
anybody went over the hill and far away. January puddles have ice at the rim
and she will stamp every one of them.

> 👶 **Infant Integration:** Kreston is 7 months. Prop him with a cushion on
> the far side of the hill, facing the basket, so every duck rises over the top
> and drops out of sight. Gone and back is the funniest thing available to him
> this month. On the duck line he babbles back at the quacking, so leave him a
> gap between verses.

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

### 🎨 Second Main Event: The Rhyme Bag

One object per rhyme goes into a pillowcase, and the bag decides what the house
sings.

**🧰 Complete Materials List:**
- 1 pillowcase or tote bag
- 6–8 household objects, one per rhyme: a spoon, a card star, a rolled sock, a watch, a plastic cup, an egg cup, one of the cardboard ducks
- 1 sheet of card and 1 marker for picture labels

**📝 Step-by-Step Instructions:**
1. Gather the objects and give each one its rhyme out loud. The spoon ran away with the dish, the mouse ran up the watch, the sock is Diddle Diddle Dumpling.
2. Draw a picture label on card for each one, rough and fast, and lay it beside its object.
3. Everything into the pillowcase. She reaches in without looking, feels about, pulls one out.
4. Whatever comes out, the whole house sings it. No skipping and no swapping.
5. Let her guess by feel before she looks, one guess out loud.
6. Once the bag is empty, lay the objects in the order they arrived and sing the set through as one medley.
7. Refill it and hand her the bag. She runs it and you sing whatever she pulls.

> 💡 **Tip:** Slip in one object with no rhyme attached, a shoe or an apple, and let her invent something when it comes out. Her version goes in the bag with the rest.

### 🎯 Afternoon Alternatives
- Three objects, one bag, sat on the bed.
- Line the objects up and let her set the order you sing them in.
- Hide the five ducks round the room and sing a verse for each one found.
- Draw the labels and stop there; the bag can wait until tomorrow.

### 🌳 Out Again: **Pick one**
- **Feeding the ducks** at the pond, with oats or defrosted peas rather than bread
- The park, for the swings and a shorter walk home
- A long loop with the pram if the pond is frozen over
- The playground with the tunnel, out of the wind
- A drive to somewhere with water, and stay in the warm car

> 👶 **Kreston's Afternoon:** Wrap him well for the pond. At 7 months a bank
> full of birds moving against grey water is the biggest thing he has been
> shown all week. Keep him off the ground at the edge. Twenty minutes in that
> cold is plenty.

---

> 🧠 **A Little Parenting Insight**
> **Why she wants the same rhyme forty times.**
> The fortieth Five Little Ducks is not the same event for her that it is for
> you. The first few passes take everything she has to follow the tune. Once
> the tune runs on its own, she has room to hear the words. Once the words are
> free, she notices the number dropping by one every verse, and after that she
> finds the joke in the last line. Repetition buys her the spare capacity to
> attend to the next layer down. Research on toddler word learning keeps
> landing in the same place: a few books read over and over beat a wide
> rotation for what stays. Sing it again.

> ⚠️ **Safety:** January pond water is cold enough to matter and the edge is
> muddy and steep. Hold her hand at the water, throw the food from well back,
> and stay between both children and the bank. Geese take fingers with the
> bread. The pegs and the small rhyme-bag objects are choke-sized, so they come
> off the floor before Kreston does.
