## 🌟 Day 25: The Memory Tray 🌟
**📅 Monday, January 25, 2027**
**Theme:** Puzzles & Problem Solving

### 🌙 Prep Tonight
> Cut sixteen palm-sized cards from the week's offcuts, leave them in a shoebox lid with the crayons, and put a clean tray and a tea towel on the table.

### ⏰ At-a-Glance Schedule
- **8:00–9:00 AM** — 🌅 Opening Activity: Filling the Tray
- **9:00–9:15 AM** — Morning Meeting (15 min)
- **9:15–10:15 AM** — 🎨 The Main Event: What's Missing from the Tray (60 min)
- **10:15–10:30 AM** — Snack (15 min)
- **10:30–11:15 AM** — 🌳 Get Outside: She Leads the Way Home (45 min)
- **11:15–11:45 AM** — Free Play (30 min)
- **11:45 AM–12:00 PM** — 🤸 Wiggle Time (15 min)
- **12:00–12:30 PM** — Lunch
- **12:30–1:00 PM** — 📚 Book Time
- **1:00–2:30 PM** — 🎨 Second Main Event: The Pairs She Draws Herself (90 min)
- **2:30–3:30 PM** — 🌳 Out Again: **Pick one** (60 min)
- **3:30–4:00 PM** — Snack & Free Play
- **4:00–4:15 PM** — 🧹 Her Job: Pairing the Socks
- **4:15–5:00 PM** — Quiet Play: The Pairs Cards
- **5:00–5:45 PM** — 🛁 Wind-Down: Two Books on the Sofa
- **6:00 PM** — **Dinner**

### 🌅 Opening Activity: Filling the Tray

**🧰 You need:** 1 tray · the run of the house · 1 tea towel.

1. Hand her the tray and send her round the house for eight things bigger than her fist. You veto nothing that fits the rule.
2. Lay them out in two rows of four and name each one out loud. Let her shuffle them until it looks right to her.
3. Cover the tray with the tea towel and leave it on the table. Say nothing about what happens next.

### 🎨 The Main Event: What's Missing from the Tray

**🧰 Complete Materials List:**
- 1 tray
- 8–10 household objects bigger than her fist: mug, wooden spoon, hairbrush, sock, apple, board book, sponge, shoe
- 1 tea towel to cover the tray
- Paper and a pencil for keeping score

**📝 Step-by-Step Instructions:**
1. Uncover the tray and name all eight, pointing at each. Give her a slow count of ten.
2. Cover it. She turns round or shuts her eyes. Take one object off and sit on it.
3. Uncover and let her name what has gone. If she stalls, name the ones still there and let her hear the gap.
4. Drop to six objects if eight is too many, go up to ten when eight stops being work.
5. Swap over. She takes one while you look away, and you work it out loud: *sock, apple, book, and the spoon was next to the book*.
6. Keep score in tally marks, hers and yours, and leave the tray out so she can run it on whoever walks in.

> 💡 **Tip:** Let her win most of them. A three-year-old who is getting them
> right plays for forty minutes. One stretched every round leaves after four.

### 🌳 Get Outside: She Leads the Way Home

Walk ten minutes out from the door, taking three or four turns, then hand the
job over: she gets you home. Name what you pass on the way out, the red door,
the postbox, the dog behind the fence. January sun sits low enough to change
how the street looks coming back, so expect one wrong turn and let her walk
into it.

> 👶 **Infant Integration:** Kreston is 7 months and working on Azlyn's
> question: does a thing still exist when nobody can see it. Play peekaboo
> with the tea towel over your face, then over a toy. Give him the wooden
> spoon off the tray and let him drop it from the high chair. He is checking
> that it lands every time, and it does.

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

### 🎨 Second Main Event: The Pairs She Draws Herself

Sixteen blank cards, eight drawings done twice, and a memory game she made
herself.

**🧰 Complete Materials List:**
- Card offcuts cut into 16 palm-sized cards
- Crayons
- 1 marker
- 1 shoebox lid to keep them in

**📝 Step-by-Step Instructions:**
1. Lay the sixteen cards out blank side up and tell her the job: eight things, each drawn twice, so every card has a twin.
2. Start her off. Draw a red circle on one card, hand her the second, and let her copy it. Wobbly copies are fine.
3. She picks the other seven. A fish, a sun, a spot, whatever comes. Draw one of each pair yourself if her hand gets tired.
4. Go over the faint crayon lines in marker so they read from across the table.
5. Play with four pairs first. Eight cards face down in two rows, turn two over, and put them back the same way up in the same place if they do not match.
6. Add pairs as she holds it. Twelve cards, then all sixteen. Stop adding the round before she starts guessing at random.
7. Everything goes in the shoebox lid and stays out for 4:15. She plays it again on her own with the cards face up.

> 💡 **Tip:** Put the cards back in the exact spot they came from. Half of this game is her remembering *where*.

### 🎯 Afternoon Alternatives
- Six cards, three pairs, played on the sofa.
- Match the pairs face up as a sorting game and skip the memory part.
- Draw the eight things on paper instead and cut them out together.
- Play snap with the finished cards instead of pairs.

### 🌳 Out Again: **Pick one**
- **Green space and a ball**, the Monday slot, and a ball is warmer than a bench
- The neighbourhood park if the grass is under water
- A walk with one job: find five things you saw this morning
- The big field, for running in a straight line until she stops
- Round the block on the scooter while the light holds

> 👶 **Kreston's Afternoon:** Give him two of anything identical, two wooden
> spoons or two cups, one for each hand. At 7 months he holds both and studies
> them, and the banging-together stage is weeks off. On the grass, sit
> him on a folded blanket where he can watch the ball go past. He turns his
> whole body after it.

---

> 🧠 **A Little Parenting Insight**
> **Working memory is the thing she is building.**
> The tray game runs on the same system Azlyn uses to follow you round the
> house: working memory, the part that holds a few things in mind while doing
> something with them. At three she can hold two or three items, which is why
> *go upstairs, get your socks and bring down the blue cup* arrives as one
> item, and it is seldom the right one. The tray gives the same system a
> workout she volunteered for. Eight objects sit above her
> ceiling, so she leans on the layout and the gap in the rows, which is memory
> strategy showing up early.

> ⚠️ **Safety:** Count the sixteen cards into the shoebox lid and out of it
> every time, and keep the tray of objects up at table height while Kreston is
> on the floor. Sweep under the table before he goes down. Crayons snap into
> mouth-sized pieces, so the broken halves go in the bin as they happen, and
> the wooden spoon he drops off the high chair is the only thing off that tray
> he gets.

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

<a id="day-26"></a>

## 🌟 Day 26: Two Ways to Sort 🌟
**📅 Tuesday, January 26, 2027**
**Theme:** Puzzles & Problem Solving

### 🌙 Prep Tonight
> Fill a laundry basket with twenty or thirty mixed things, take the knives out of the cutlery drawer, and put a biscuit or stickers aside for tomorrow.

### ⏰ At-a-Glance Schedule
- **8:00–9:00 AM** — 🌅 Opening Activity: The Basket of Everything
- **9:00–9:15 AM** — Morning Meeting (15 min)
- **9:15–10:15 AM** — 🎨 The Main Event: Sorting It Twice (60 min)
- **10:15–10:30 AM** — Snack (15 min)
- **10:30–11:15 AM** — 🌳 Get Outside: Two Bags, One Rule (45 min)
- **11:15–11:45 AM** — Free Play (30 min)
- **11:45 AM–12:00 PM** — 🤸 Wiggle Time (15 min)
- **12:00–12:30 PM** — Lunch
- **12:30–1:00 PM** — 📚 Book Time
- **1:00–2:30 PM** — 🎨 Second Main Event: Every Lid Has a Box (90 min)
- **2:30–3:30 PM** — 🌳 Out Again: **Swimming** (60 min)
- **3:30–4:00 PM** — Snack & Free Play
- **4:00–4:15 PM** — 🧹 Her Job: The Forks
- **4:15–5:00 PM** — Quiet Play: The Sorting Bowls
- **5:00–5:45 PM** — 🛁 Wind-Down: After Swimming, Straight to Pyjamas
- **6:00 PM** — **Dinner**

### 🌅 Opening Activity: The Basket of Everything

**🧰 You need:** the full basket · 4 bowls or paper bags · masking tape and a marker.

1. Tip the basket out on the floor and sit in it with her. Half of it she has not seen for weeks and she will greet all of it.
2. Ask her to put two things together that go together. Take whatever reason she gives, including *they are both Kreston's*. Three or four times.
3. Line the four bowls up beside the pile and say nothing about what goes in them. The sorting starts at 9:15.

### 🎨 The Main Event: Sorting It Twice

**🧰 Complete Materials List:**
- 1 basket of 20–30 mixed household objects and toys
- 4 bowls or paper bags
- Paper and a marker for labels
- Masking tape

**📝 Step-by-Step Instructions:**
1. Four bowls, four colours. Tape a coloured scrap to each or write the colour word on it. She sorts the whole pile, and the two-colour ones are hers to call.
2. Walk her along the four bowls and count each one out loud. Announce the winner.
3. Tip the lot back into one pile.
4. Same objects, new rule: things that need two hands, things that fit in one. Two bowls. She will complain about starting again, then beat her own time.
5. Third round is hers. She sorts by a rule she keeps secret and you work it out from what lands in each bowl. Guess wrong twice before you get there.
6. Her rule will be *things I like* and *things I do not*. Both count. Write it on the paper, read it back and tape it to the bowl.

> 💡 **Tip:** The moment worth waiting for is the second sort, when a red spoon
> that was a red thing an hour ago becomes a two-hands thing. Say so out loud.

### 🌳 Get Outside: Two Bags, One Rule

Two paper bags and one rule set at the front gate: things that came off a tree
in the left bag, things that never did in the right. Late January hands over
brown leaves, bare twigs, a seed head, a bottle top, gravel. The arguments are
the good part. Ten minutes of collecting is plenty in this cold.

> 👶 **Infant Integration:** Kreston is 7 months. Give him his own bowl with
> two objects too big to swallow and watch what he does. He takes them out and
> does not put them back, because putting things into a container comes around
> nine months. Today he empties, you refill, and he thinks that is the game.

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

### 🎨 Second Main Event: Every Lid Has a Box

The kitchen floor gets the version with a real result, and by six she has
fixed two things you meant to fix in November.

**🧰 Complete Materials List:**
- All the tupperware boxes and lids
- The cutlery drawer, **knives removed first**
- 1 tea towel
- 1 washing-up bowl
- Masking tape and a marker for labelling shelves

**📝 Step-by-Step Instructions:**
1. Take every knife and skewer out of the drawer and onto the top of the fridge before she comes into the kitchen.
2. Tip all the tupperware onto the floor. Lids in one heap, boxes in another, a metre apart.
3. She matches. Hand her the three most obvious, then stand back. A lid that almost fits is the best part of the job.
4. Any box with no lid goes in the washing-up bowl, out of the house or into the craft pile. She makes the call.
5. Stack the matched boxes biggest at the bottom and put them back on the shelf. Tape a label on the shelf edge and let her say what to write.
6. Now the drawer. Tip it onto the tea towel and let her sort each piece back into its slot: forks, spoons, teaspoons, a bowl for the odd stuff.
7. Leave the drawer open. She checks her own work at 4:00 when she does the forks.

> 💡 **Tip:** Do not re-sort behind her tonight. She walks past that drawer
> forty times this week, and every time she opens it she sees something she
> got right.

### 🎯 Afternoon Alternatives
- The lids only, and stop when the tupperware is done.
- Sort the toy basket into two boxes, one of them for the charity shop.
- Pair the shoes by the front door, all facing the same way.

### 🌳 Out Again: **Swimming**
- **Swimming**, the Tuesday anchor, and the fourth of the year
- The indoor play centre if the pool is shut
- A warm café and a window seat, if nobody has it in them
- Home, a warm bath instead, and an extra book at five

> 👶 **Kreston's Afternoon:** He comes too if he is up for it. At 7 months he
> sits supported in the water and splashes with intent. The pool hall throws
> his own voice back at him, so he shouts at the ceiling and waits to hear it
> again. Hold him chest to chest at first, twenty minutes and out before he
> cools.

---

> 🧠 **A Little Parenting Insight**
> **Classification is quiet, invisible math.**
> Before Azlyn can count six forks she has to decide what a fork is and that
> these six are all one kind of thing. Sorting is where that gets settled.
> Every bowl she fills is a set, every argument about the two-colour ones is
> her testing where the edge of a category sits, and number is built on top of
> that. The second sort does something further. Running a new rule over the
> same objects, colour one hour and size the next, is the flexible half of the
> job, and it is harder than the first sort. You have her pairing socks and
> sorting cutlery already. Same task, real result.

> ⚠️ **Safety:** Knives, skewers and anything with a point come out of the
> drawer before she is in the room and stay out until it goes back. Small
> sorted objects stay off the floor while Kreston is down there, and the
> basket goes up on the table between rounds. Never leave the drawer open at
> her height with the sharp things still in it.

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

<a id="day-27"></a>

## 🌟 Day 27: Describe It First 🌟
**📅 Wednesday, January 27, 2027**
**Theme:** Puzzles & Problem Solving

### 🌙 Prep Tonight
> Leave an empty pillowcase and a clean tray on the table, hide the biscuit or the stickers, and check you have two rolls of masking tape for tomorrow.

### ⏰ At-a-Glance Schedule
- **8:00–9:00 AM** — 🌅 Opening Activity: Choosing the Secret Objects
- **9:00–9:15 AM** — Morning Meeting (15 min)
- **9:15–10:15 AM** — 🎨 The Main Event: The Bag You Can't See Into (60 min)
- **10:15–10:30 AM** — Snack (15 min)
- **10:30–11:15 AM** — 🌳 Get Outside: Eyes Shut, Five Sounds (45 min)
- **11:15–11:45 AM** — Free Play (30 min)
- **11:45 AM–12:00 PM** — 🤸 Wiggle Time (15 min)
- **12:00–12:30 PM** — Lunch
- **12:30–1:00 PM** — 📚 Book Time
- **1:00–2:30 PM** — 🎨 Second Main Event: Picture Clues (90 min)
- **2:30–3:30 PM** — 🌳 Out Again: **Pick one** (60 min)
- **3:30–4:00 PM** — Snack & Free Play
- **4:00–4:15 PM** — 🧹 Her Job: The Forks
- **4:15–5:00 PM** — Quiet Play: The Mystery Bag on Her Own
- **5:00–5:45 PM** — 🛁 Wind-Down: Torchlight and One Long Book
- **6:00 PM** — **Dinner**

### 🌅 Opening Activity: Choosing the Secret Objects

**🧰 You need:** 1 pillowcase or tote bag · a wander round the house · 1 tray.

1. Send her round for things that feel different, one out of every room. Rough, cold, furry, prickly, smooth.
2. Feel each one before it goes in, hands only and eyes on the ceiling. Give her three words for it and ask for three back.
3. Post them all into the pillowcase and stand it where she can see it and not reach in. She will try anyway.

### 🎨 The Main Event: The Bag You Can't See Into

**🧰 Complete Materials List:**
- 1 pillowcase or tote bag
- 8–10 objects with different textures, all bigger than a fist: orange, hairbrush, scrubbing brush, wooden spoon, flannel, shoe, mug, bath sponge
- 1 tray to lay the guessed ones on

**📝 Step-by-Step Instructions:**
1. Sit opposite each other with the bag between you. One rule: hand goes in, eyes stay out.
2. Whoever's turn it is finds one object and says three things about it before anybody guesses. *Hard, cold, round.*
3. Go first so she hears what three things sounds like. Take your time and be wrong at the end.
4. Her turn. She will blurt the answer out, at least twice. Ask for one more thing and let her hold it while she thinks.
5. Objects come out onto the tray as they are named. When the bag is empty, cover it and see how many she can list.
6. Last round the other way: you describe one from memory, hands on the table, and she finds it in the bag by feel.

> 💡 **Tip:** Give her the words she is missing while her hand is on the object. *Bumpy* and *ridged* and *cold at one end* land now and vanish later.

### 🌳 Get Outside: Eyes Shut, Five Sounds

Stop on the path, both shut your eyes, and count five sounds off on her
fingers. There are no leaves this week so sound carries: a crow, a car, a dog,
a plane, your own boots on the grit. Do it again by a road and behind a hedge,
and ask which was louder. She points at the road with her eyes still shut.

> 👶 **Infant Integration:** Kreston is 7 months and his mouth is still his
> best instrument for finding out what a thing is. Give him a flannel, a wooden
> spoon and a silicone spatula, one at a time. Put words on each while he
> works it over. Keep the bath sponge in the bag and out of his reach,
> because it comes apart in bits.

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

### 🎨 Second Main Event: Picture Clues

Six drawings, taped round the house, each sending her to where the next one
waits.

**🧰 Complete Materials List:**
- Paper cut into 6 cards
- Crayons
- Masking tape
- 1 small prize: a biscuit or a sticker
- 1 teddy to hide

**📝 Step-by-Step Instructions:**
1. Hide teddy first with the prize under him, somewhere she walks past twenty times a day.
2. Draw six clues, one per card: the bath, the fridge, her boots, the sofa, the kitchen bin, and last of all teddy's spot. Rough drawings are fine.
3. Tape clue one where she will start. Every card sends her to the place drawn on it, where the next waits.
4. Walk it with her and carry nothing. She holds the card, she decides where the drawing means, she gets to be wrong about the fridge.
5. She finds teddy, gets the prize and eats it standing up in the hall.
6. Now she hides teddy and draws your six clues. No help with the drawings, however long they take.
7. Follow her clues as drawn, including the one that makes no sense. Ask what it is and take the answer she gives.

> 💡 **Tip:** She will want to tell you where teddy is about four clues in. Ask her to whisper it to the wall and go on hunting. Holding a secret for ten minutes is work.

### 🎯 Afternoon Alternatives
- Three clues instead of six, run in the kitchen alone.
- Hot and cold, called out loud, with no cards at all.
- Hide the same object ten times and get faster each round.
- Draw a map of the downstairs and mark an X on it.

### 🌳 Out Again: **Pick one**
- **The indoor play centre**, the Wednesday slot, and it is too cold for the park
- Soft play with the quiet session, if the big one is full of school-run crowds
- The library, a week early, and take out something with flaps in it
- The shops, and let her carry one thing and pay for it
- A friend's house with the mystery bag in your coat pocket

> 👶 **Kreston's Afternoon:** The baby area at the play centre is his. Sit him
> in the ball pit shallows with a hand on his back, because at 7 months he
> sits well but tips at speed. Mirrors at his level beat any of the toys.
> Twenty minutes, then out to somewhere quieter.

---

> 🧠 **A Little Parenting Insight**
> **Describing before guessing is the whole skill.**
> Guessing is fast and closes the thinking down. Describing keeps it open
> and costs her more, because she has to find words for weight, temperature,
> texture and shape while her hand is the only thing telling her anything.
> Every one of those words is a category she can carry to the next object.
> Teachers call this attribute language, and children with plenty of it find
> it easier later to explain how they got to an answer, which is most of what
> school asks them for. The rule you set at the top, three things before
> anybody guesses, is what does the work.

> ⚠️ **Safety:** The bath sponge comes apart in bits, so it goes back in the
> bag between turns and the bag stays off the floor. Hide the biscuit
> somewhere Kreston cannot get to, and let Azlyn eat it before you put him
> down. Press the tape ends flat on every clue you stick up at his height, and
> pull them all off the walls once the hunt is over.

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

<a id="day-28"></a>

## 🌟 Day 28: Dead Ends 🌟
**📅 Thursday, January 28, 2027**
**Theme:** Puzzles & Problem Solving

### 🌙 Prep Tonight
> Flatten the last cereal boxes and stand them in the baking tray with the masking tape and the ping-pong ball. Scissors up out of reach.

### ⏰ At-a-Glance Schedule
- **8:00–9:00 AM** — 🌅 Opening Activity: Cutting the Maze Walls
- **9:00–9:15 AM** — Morning Meeting (15 min)
- **9:15–10:15 AM** — 🎨 The Main Event: The Maze in a Tray (60 min)
- **10:15–10:30 AM** — Snack (15 min)
- **10:30–11:15 AM** — 🌳 Get Outside: She Picks Every Turning (45 min)
- **11:15–11:45 AM** — Free Play (30 min)
- **11:45 AM–12:00 PM** — 🤸 Wiggle Time (15 min)
- **12:00–12:30 PM** — Lunch
- **12:30–1:00 PM** — 📚 Book Time
- **1:00–2:30 PM** — 🎨 Second Main Event: The Maze You Walk (90 min)
- **2:30–3:30 PM** — 🌳 Out Again: **Pick one** (60 min)
- **3:30–4:00 PM** — Snack & Free Play
- **4:00–4:15 PM** — 🧹 Her Job: The Forks
- **4:15–5:00 PM** — Quiet Play: Teddy Does the Maze
- **5:00–5:45 PM** — 🛁 Wind-Down: The Week's Puzzles, Laid Out
- **6:00 PM** — **Dinner**

### 🌅 Opening Activity: Cutting the Maze Walls

**🧰 You need:** cereal-box card · scissors (adult only) · 1 baking tray · masking tape.

1. Cut the card into strips two fingers wide. She counts them into a pile as they come off, and you want about fifteen.
2. Fold each strip along its length so it stands on its own edge. Let her fold all fifteen and leave the crooked ones crooked.
3. Stand a few loose in the baking tray and let her shove them round with one finger. Nothing gets taped down until 9:15.

### 🎨 The Main Event: The Maze in a Tray

**🧰 Complete Materials List:**
- 1 baking tray or shallow box lid
- Cereal-box card cut into strips
- 1 roll masking tape
- Scissors (**adult only**)
- 1 ping-pong ball, or a tight ball of foil
- 1 marker

**📝 Step-by-Step Instructions:**
1. Draw the route on paper first. Start at one corner, finish at the other, four turns between. Mark three dead ends and let her put an X on each.
2. Tape the first walls into the tray along the route you drew, gaps wide enough for the ball.
3. Build in the three dead ends. Say nothing about them at all.
4. Give her the ball and let her tilt. She will hit the first dead end inside ten seconds and look up at you. Look back and keep quiet.
5. Once she has all three worked out, hand her the tape and tell her the maze is hers to change. She will open the dead ends up.
6. Last run on the floor, tray held in two hands, standing over it. Time her, then let her time you and be slower.

> 💡 **Tip:** Keep the walls low enough to see over. She has to watch the ball
> go the wrong way to know it went wrong, and a tall wall turns it into luck.

### 🌳 Get Outside: She Picks Every Turning

Out the front door and every junction is hers. She calls left or right, you
walk it, no steering and no hints. Turn back at twenty minutes and let her get
you home. Some of her turns are dead ends, somebody's driveway or a fence, and
those are the good ones. The cold sits in her hands this week, so mittens on.

> 👶 **Infant Integration:** Kreston is 7 months. The ping-pong ball and the
> foil stay up on the table, so give him something too big to swallow: a
> football, or a rolled-up towel. Roll it across him at walking pace so he
> follows it over his own midline and reaches across. He may push himself
> backwards trying and end up under the sofa, which is how a lot of babies
> move first.

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

### 🎨 Second Main Event: The Maze You Walk

Tape on the floor, cushions for corners, teddy at the finish, and this time the
thing going through the maze is her.

**🧰 Complete Materials List:**
- 2 rolls masking tape
- 4 sofa cushions
- 2 dining chairs
- 1 teddy for the end
- Paper and a marker to draw the plan first

**📝 Step-by-Step Instructions:**
1. Draw the plan together on paper. She can copy the tray maze or invent a new one, but three dead ends go on it first.
2. Clear one room end to end. Lay the tape two lines at a time so she watches a corridor appear rather than a finished puzzle.
3. Cushions and chair legs go on the corners where tape on the floor stops reading as a wall.
4. Teddy waits at the finish. She starts on the taped X by the door.
5. First run she walks it. Second run on all fours, third on tiptoe, fourth carrying teddy back the other way without touching a wall.
6. Hand her the tape and let her turn one dead end into a door.
7. Leave it down for the evening and make everybody walk it to get to dinner, including whoever comes in at six.

> 💡 **Tip:** Run it once yourself, out loud, saying what you think at each
> turn. *That is a wall, so I am going back to the last corner.* She takes the
> words for her next run.

### 🎯 Afternoon Alternatives
- One straight tape corridor with two turns and nothing else.
- Drive toy cars round the tray maze instead of rolling the ball.
- Draw a maze on paper and let her find the way out with a finger.

### 🌳 Out Again: **Pick one**
- **The post office**, with a letter she posts herself
- The hardware shop, for the aisles that go on forever
- The grocery shop, and she navigates to the bananas
- The playground on the way home, twenty minutes

> 👶 **Kreston's Afternoon:** Put him at the finish next to teddy, so Azlyn's
> arrival is the reward for the pair of them. He is 7 months and picking at
> edges with his fingers, so press the tape ends flat and pull up anything
> that has lifted. In the post office queue, hand him something to hold before
> he decides for himself.

---

> 🧠 **A Little Parenting Insight**
> **Dead ends are the point of a maze.**
> A maze with no wrong turns in it is a corridor. The three dead ends make
> Azlyn stop, look at where she has got to, decide the route was wrong and go
> back. That is a different job from pushing harder at the same thing.
> Psychologists call dropping a plan that is not working cognitive
> flexibility, and three is about when it arrives. Her first move at a dead
> end will be to shove the ball into the wall again. Wait. The second thing
> she tries is the one worth having, and you gave her a wrong turn she can
> survive, with nothing riding on it.

> ⚠️ **Safety:** The ping-pong ball and any foil balls or dried beans in the
> tray are a choking hazard for Kreston. Build at table height, count the ball
> back in at the end of every run, and sweep before he goes down on the floor.
> The scissors are adult only and go back up out of reach between cuts.

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
<a id="week-5"></a>

## Week 5: Forest Friends 🌲
**📅 January 29 – February 4, 2027**

---

### 🎯 What This Week Is About

Azlyn prints four animal coats on Friday morning and builds the animals after
lunch, then spends the rest of the week finding places for them to live. A
tree up the living room wall on Saturday, a forest floor in a baking tray on
Sunday, tracks across the hall on Monday, feeders outside the window on
Tuesday, a cave in a cardboard box on Wednesday.
Every day inherits the day before it. By Thursday she has a forest in the
house and enough of it in her head to tell the whole week back to you through
a lit sheet.

February arrives on Monday, and Groundhog Day lands on Tuesday the 2nd as a
bonus note inside the afternoon: people in America gather round a hole to
watch a woodchuck come up and check the weather. It sits an inch from
Wednesday's hibernation cave, so the two can talk to each other. The cold does
the rest of the work this week. Frost on the grass until the sun comes round
the houses, bare branches with every old nest showing, and shadows that run
long at eleven in the morning.

---

### 🧒 What Azlyn Will Learn

- **Patterns she can carry on.** Spot, spot, stripe down a paper strip, then finishing the row without you.
- **Where forest animals live.** Roots, trunk, branches, top, and which animal belongs at which height.
- **Reading evidence.** A print, a chewed leaf and a tuft of wool add up to an animal she never saw.
- **Waiting, and holding still on purpose.** She packs four feeders on Tuesday and the birds come on Thursday; she hibernates on Wednesday while you count to twenty.
- **Telling the week back.** Two tours, a puppet show, and a shadow fox that swells up the sheet as she walks him toward the torch.

---

### 👩 What You'll Learn

- Patterns are the beginning of algebra
- Explaining to someone else is how understanding gets locked in
- Loose parts beat finished toys
- Evidence and inference, a big idea learnable at three
- Delayed payoff is a muscle, and this is a gentle way to work it
- Pretending to sleep is real self-regulation practice
- Shadows are confusing at three, and the confusion is worth exploring

---

### 🛒 This Week's Shopping List

> *Everything both main events call for, all seven days. Check what you've
> already got before Friday.*

- [ ] Cardboard tubes, 10–12 (start saving now)
- [ ] One cardboard box big enough for Azlyn to sit up inside
- [ ] A second box to flatten for the tree trunk
- [ ] Cereal boxes or thick card, 4–5
- [ ] Brown paper bags, 4–6, or wrapping paper with a plain back
- [ ] Carrier bags for collecting, 2–3
- [ ] Newspaper, a stack
- [ ] Old magazines or green paper, for leaves
- [ ] Card scraps for ears, tails and beaks
- [ ] Plain paper, a good stack
- [ ] Dark paper or a dark towel
- [ ] Masking tape, 3 rolls
- [ ] String, 1 ball
- [ ] Washable non-toxic poster paint in brown, black, white and orange
- [ ] Saucers or old plates, 4
- [ ] Crayons, one of them fat enough to rub on its side
- [ ] A permanent marker
- [ ] A pencil
- [ ] Chalk
- [ ] Scissors and a skewer (adult only)
- [ ] Lolly sticks, straws or spare pencils
- [ ] Corks, 6–8
- [ ] Clothes pegs or bulldog clips, 8
- [ ] Bottle tops
- [ ] Cotton wool and fabric scraps
- [ ] A tuft of wool, for a trail clue
- [ ] A jar lid
- [ ] A baking tray, the biggest you own, or a large shallow box lid
- [ ] Soil or sand, a few handfuls
- [ ] Small stones and twigs from the garden
- [ ] A jug and a spray bottle
- [ ] Plain flour, 1 kg
- [ ] A sieve
- [ ] A sponge, for the paw stamps
- [ ] A dustpan and brush
- [ ] Solid vegetable fat or lard, 250 g
- [ ] Birdseed, 3 cups, or porridge oats with breadcrumbs and raisins
- [ ] Empty yoghurt pots, 4, or pine cones
- [ ] A mixing bowl and a wooden spoon
- [ ] A fork and a roll of kitchen roll
- [ ] A flannel and a shallow bowl
- [ ] A torch, with fresh batteries and a spare set
- [ ] A pan lid
- [ ] A white or pale sheet
- [ ] Blankets, 2, and 2 cushions
- [ ] Milk and a snack, for eating in the cave
- [ ] Books about bears or winter, 2–3
- [ ] Toy animals, in case the Forest Family does not come off
- [ ] Your phone, charged

---

### 💛 A Note from Joseph

> *(Draft. Rewrite this. I can guess the shape of it, not the feeling.)*
>
> Brooklyn, this week has me in it twice and I am not in the room for either.
> Saturday you record the forest tour and send it to me, and Monday she lays a
> trail across the floor so I can follow it in at six with the lights down. I
> know what both of those cost you. An afternoon of flour on a hard floor, and
> a video you had to remember to take with Kreston on your hip. She will never
> notice I was missing from those hours, and that is your doing. Leave the
> animals on the shelf where she put them. I want to be shown them by her, in
> her order, with the wrong ears still on.

---

> 👶 **Kreston is 7 months this week**. He reaches across his body for things
> set out to one side, passes them from hand to hand, and babbles in long
> strings that carry the rhythm of sentences and none of the words. **He turns
> 8 months on Friday the 5th, the day after this week ends.**

---

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

<a id="day-29"></a>

## 🌟 Day 29: The Animal Coats 🌟
**📅 Friday, January 29, 2027**
**Theme:** Forest Friends

### 🌙 Prep Tonight
> Cut six long strips from a paper bag or the back of the wrapping paper, and stand the paints, four saucers and a stack of newspaper at one end of the table.

### ⏰ At-a-Glance Schedule
- **8:00–9:00 AM** — 🌅 Opening Activity: The Stamp Hunt
- **9:00–9:15 AM** — Morning Meeting (15 min)
- **9:15–10:15 AM** — 🎨 The Main Event: Printing the Animal Coats (60 min)
- **10:15–10:30 AM** — Snack (15 min)
- **10:30–11:15 AM** — 🌳 Get Outside: The Bark Rubbing Walk (45 min)
- **11:15–11:45 AM** — Free Play (30 min)
- **11:45 AM–12:00 PM** — 🤸 Wiggle Time (15 min)
- **12:00–12:30 PM** — Lunch
- **12:30–1:00 PM** — 📚 Book Time
- **1:00–2:30 PM** — 🎨 Second Main Event: Building the Forest Family (90 min)
- **2:30–3:30 PM** — 🌳 Out Again: **Pick one** (60 min)
- **3:30–4:00 PM** — Snack & Free Play
- **4:00–4:15 PM** — 🧹 Her Job: The Forks
- **4:15–5:00 PM** — Quiet Play: The Animal Line-Up
- **5:00–5:45 PM** — 🛁 Wind-Down: Bath and a Forest Story
- **6:00 PM** — **Dinner**

### 🌅 Opening Activity: The Stamp Hunt

**🧰 You need:** 1 carrier bag · the recycling · the odds drawer.

1. Hand her the bag and one job: find things that make a mark. A cork, a fork, a cotton reel, a tube end, a bottle top.
2. Press each find into your palm and ask what shape it would leave. Take her guesses without fixing them.
3. Line the winners along the table edge in the order she wants to use them. That is the plan for 9:15.

### 🎨 The Main Event: Printing the Animal Coats

**🧰 Complete Materials List:**
- Long paper strips cut from a paper bag or the back of wrapping paper, 6
- Washable non-toxic poster paint in brown, black, white and orange
- 1 saucer or old plate for each colour
- Cardboard tube ends, 1 cork, 1 fork, 1 scrunched piece of kitchen roll
- 1 wet flannel in a shallow bowl
- Newspaper for the table

**📝 Step-by-Step Instructions:**
1. Spread the newspaper, lay one strip in front of her the long way, and pour a thin puddle of each colour into its own saucer.
2. Start the fawn. Two spots with the cork, a stripe dragged with the back of the fork, two spots again. Say it as you print: *spot, spot, stripe.* Hand her the cork on the third repeat.
3. Badger next. Wide black and white bands from a tube squashed flat, alternating down the strip. She takes over after two.
4. Owl on the third strip: rings from the open end of a tube, rows of them, brown on brown.
5. Fox on the fourth: orange the whole length with the scrunched kitchen roll, then a white tip.
6. Hand her the last two strips with no pattern to follow. Those two are hers, printed however she likes. Lay all six flat to dry, wash hands, then walk the row and ask what comes next on the four you started.

> 💡 **Tip:** Print the first repeat and stop. She finishes the strip. A pattern she completed is one she can read back at you an hour later.

### 🌳 Get Outside: The Bark Rubbing Walk

Take paper and a fat crayon with the wrapper peeled off. Rub the crayon on its
side over paper held flat against four different trunks. Oak comes out deep
and ridged, birch close to smooth, and she feels the difference through the
paper before she sees it. January bark holds the wet, so work the sheltered
side of each trunk.

> 👶 **Infant Integration:** Kreston is 7 months and passes things from hand
> to hand now. Give him a wooden spoon and a folded flannel, the second set out
> to his side so he has to reach across his body for it. He keeps up a running
> commentary at both of them while Azlyn prints. On the walk, hold his open palm
> flat against a trunk for a second.

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

### 🎨 Second Main Event: Building the Forest Family

The strips are dry by one o'clock. Wrapped round a cardboard tube and taped,
each one stands up on the table by itself.

**🧰 Complete Materials List:**
- The printed strips
- Cardboard tubes, 4–6
- 1 roll masking tape
- Scissors (**adult**)
- Card scraps for ears, tails and beaks
- 1 permanent marker

**📝 Step-by-Step Instructions:**
1. Lay the dry strips out and ask which animal each one is. Take her answer even when the badger has turned into a zebra.
2. Wrap the fawn strip round a tube with the pattern facing out and tape the seam on the inside. She holds, you tape.
3. Cut ears, a tail and a beak from the card scraps. She draws the shape, you cut on her line.
4. Tape the pieces where she points, including the ears you would have put somewhere else.
5. Stand each animal on the table as you go so the row grows in front of her.
6. Write every name on the base in marker. Fox is a name and so is Bibby.
7. Give them an address: a shelf, a windowsill, the corner of the table. They stay there all week.

> 💡 **Tip:** Tape the seam inside the tube where her fingers cannot find it. A seam she can pick at comes apart by Sunday, and these four have a week of work ahead.

### 🎯 Afternoon Alternatives
- One strip, one tube, one animal, and stop there.
- Draw faces onto plain tubes and skip the printing.
- Sort the dried strips by colour, then by pattern.
- Line the animals up by height and photograph them.

### 🌳 Out Again: **Pick one**
- **Grandparents, or whoever in the family is nearest**, and she takes one animal to show them
- A friend's house, if the Forest Family needs an audience under five
- The park, back to the trees she rubbed this morning
- A café with a window seat and paper for one more rubbing
- A drive out to somewhere with more trees in it than houses

> 👶 **Kreston's Afternoon:** Keep him off the tape and the tube ends and give
> him a big offcut of card instead, wider than his own mouth. At 7 months he
> pulls at paper with both hands and turns it over hunting for the other side.
> He runs a long string of sound at you while he works, so answer as though he
> asked you something.

### 🌍 Around the World: Japan, *Shinrin-yoku* 🌲

In Japan, *shinrin-yoku* means forest bathing, a walk with nothing to achieve.
No route, no distance, no finish. You go in among the trees and use your
senses: what you can hear, what you can smell, how bark feels under a hand.
Japanese doctors send patients out to do it, and the walk counts as treatment.

> **✨ Try it:** Green tea and a rice ball, eaten under a tree with your coats
> on. Ten minutes of sitting and listening counts as the whole practice.

> *Did you know?* Japan's forestry ministry invented the word *shinrin-yoku* in
> 1982, and the name came before any of the science did.

---

> 🧠 **A Little Parenting Insight**
> **Patterns are the beginning of algebra.**
> Azlyn printed spot, spot, stripe, spot, spot, stripe down a paper strip this
> morning, and the work underneath it is the work a nine-year-old does with x.
> A pattern is a rule that holds while the pieces change. Once she can say
> what comes next on the badger strip, she can find the rule in a row of cups,
> a clapped rhythm or a line of numbers, because one skill sits under all
> three. Researchers who track early maths put pattern work ahead of counting
> as a predictor of where a child lands later. You never have to name any of
> it. Print the first repeat, hand her the cork, and let her carry the rule to
> the end.

> ⚠️ **Safety:** Kreston is 7 months and everything he reaches goes in his
> mouth. Corks, tube ends and card scraps go up out of reach the moment you
> stop watching, on a shelf and not the table edge. Washable non-toxic paint
> only. The scissors stay with you.

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

<a id="day-30"></a>

## 🌟 Day 30: The Tree on the Wall 🌟
**📅 Saturday, January 30, 2027**
**Theme:** Forest Friends

### 🌙 Prep Tonight
> Flatten a cardboard box or save two big paper bags for the trunk, and clear a stretch of wall from the skirting board up.

### ⏰ At-a-Glance Schedule
- **8:00–9:00 AM** — 🌅 Opening Activity: Tearing the Trunk
- **9:00–9:15 AM** — Morning Meeting (15 min)
- **9:15–10:15 AM** — 🎨 The Main Event: The Tree That Fills the Wall (60 min)
- **10:15–10:30 AM** — Snack (15 min)
- **10:30–11:15 AM** — 🌳 Get Outside: Choosing Her Tree (45 min)
- **11:15–11:45 AM** — Free Play (30 min)
- **11:45 AM–12:00 PM** — 🤸 Wiggle Time (15 min)
- **12:00–12:30 PM** — Lunch
- **12:30–1:00 PM** — 📚 Book Time
- **1:00–2:30 PM** — 🎨 Second Main Event: The Forest Tour (90 min)
- **2:30–3:30 PM** — 🌳 Out Again: **Pick one** (60 min)
- **3:30–4:00 PM** — Snack & Free Play
- **4:00–4:15 PM** — 🧹 Her Job: The Forks and the Cups
- **4:15–5:00 PM** — Quiet Play: Adding to the Tree
- **5:00–5:45 PM** — 🛁 Wind-Down: Reading Under the Branches
- **6:00 PM** — **Dinner**

### 🌅 Opening Activity: Tearing the Trunk

**🧰 You need:** brown paper bags or a flattened box · masking tape · the cleared wall.

1. Tear the paper into long ragged strips instead of cutting it. A torn edge looks like bark, and her hands can do it all.
2. Lay the strips end to end along the floor and stand her beside the line.
3. Ask where the branches should start. Tape a tab on the wall at that height and leave the pile until 9:15.

### 🎨 The Main Event: The Tree That Fills the Wall

**🧰 Complete Materials List:**
- Brown paper bags, a flattened cardboard box, or the plain side of wrapping paper
- 1 roll masking tape
- Scissors (**adult**)
- Green paper, newspaper or magazine pages for leaves
- 1 permanent marker
- The Forest Family animals, or any toy animals

**📝 Step-by-Step Instructions:**
1. Tape the torn strips to the wall as a trunk, floor to shoulder height, wider at the bottom. Overlap them until no wall shows.
2. Add branches from the shorter strips. She decides how many and where, and crooked ones stay crooked.
3. Tear leaves from the green paper or magazine pages and tape them along the branches in handfuls.
4. Draw a line along the skirting board and run roots below it. That line is the ground.
5. Sort the animals. Ask where each one lives: roots, trunk, branches, top. Tape a card shelf at that height and stand it there.
6. Write the four layers up the wall in marker: **roots, trunk, branches, top.** Read them out with her, bottom up.

> 💡 **Tip:** Build it at her height. A tree she can reach the top of is one she keeps adding to all week, and the ceiling end is yours.

### 🌳 Get Outside: Choosing Her Tree

Walk the route and let her pick one tree to be hers for the year. She has to
give a reason, and any reason counts. Put both arms round the trunk to see
whether they meet, then count the big branches. Photograph her against it
today, the last Saturday in January, and again in April, July and October.

> 👶 **Infant Integration:** Kreston is 7 months and tracks Azlyn across a
> room now. Lay him on his back under the wall tree to look up at the leaves.
> Tape one green paper leaf at the height of his hand, off to one side so he
> has to reach across his body for it. He crumples it and passes it over.

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

### 🎨 Second Main Event: The Forest Tour

She knows where every animal on that wall lives. This afternoon she says it
out loud to an audience that cannot get up and leave.

**🧰 Complete Materials List:**
- The wall tree
- 1 torch
- 1 cardboard tube, for a megaphone
- Paper scraps and 1 crayon, for tickets
- Your phone, to record one tour for Joseph
- A chair each for the teddies, and a cushion on the floor for Kreston

**📝 Step-by-Step Instructions:**
1. Set the chairs in a row facing the tree with a teddy in every one. Kreston sits at the end, propped against the cushion.
2. Make tickets. She scribbles on the paper scraps and hands one to every seat, Kreston included.
3. Give her the tube and the torch. The tube is her voice and the torch is her pointer.
4. Take a seat and be the audience. Who lives at the top. Why does nobody live in the roots. What happens up there when it rains.
5. Let her be wrong. Owls in the roots is a claim, and a claim beats a correct answer copied off you.
6. Record one tour start to finish with no retakes. Joseph gets it tonight.
7. Swap over. You give the tour, and she corrects you before you finish the first sentence.

> 💡 **Tip:** Ask for the tour twice with an hour in between. The second runs longer and adds something she worked out in the gap.

### 🎯 Afternoon Alternatives
- A tour of one animal, thirty seconds, done.
- Draw the tree on paper and label the layers together.
- Hide the animals round the room and have her put each one back at its height.
- Give the whole tour to Joseph on a video call.

### 🌳 Out Again: **Pick one**
- **The botanical garden**, glasshouse first if the wind is up
- A garden centre with an indoor plant section, the same trees in miniature
- The park with the biggest trees you know, for the shapes of them bare
- A churchyard or an arboretum, both good for old trunks in winter
- A café near the garden and a hot chocolate on the way home

> 👶 **Kreston's Afternoon:** At 7 months the wet warm air of the glasshouse,
> the smell and the change in sound as the door shuts all land at once, and he
> goes still and stares. Carry him facing out and stop at three plants, his hand
> under yours on one big rubbery leaf. Back home he props against a cushion at
> the end of the row.

---

> 🧠 **A Little Parenting Insight**
> **Explaining to someone else is how understanding gets locked in.**
> Azlyn giving the tour is doing harder work than Azlyn being told. To explain
> the tree she has to put the parts in an order, find words for the joins, and
> run into the bits she does not have. Researchers call it self-explanation,
> and children who talk their way through a problem hold on to it longer than
> children who watched a demonstration. Her audience does not need to follow a
> word. Kreston and three teddies do the job as well as an adult, because the
> value sits in the telling. Ask one real question at the end and let the
> silence run while she hunts for the answer.

> ⚠️ **Safety:** Kreston sits on the floor against a cushion for the tour and
> never on a dining chair. At 7 months he goes over sideways with no warning.
> The leaf taped at his height and every loose end of masking tape come away
> the moment you stop watching, because both go straight to his mouth. The
> scissors stay with you, up on a shelf between cuts.

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
