## 🌟 Day 31: The Forest Floor 🌟
**📅 Sunday, January 31, 2027**
**Theme:** Forest Friends

### 🌙 Prep Tonight
> Empty the odds drawer into a bowl on the table (corks, pegs, bottle tops, cotton wool, fabric scraps) and dig out the biggest baking tray you own.

### ⏰ At-a-Glance Schedule
- **8:00–9:00 AM** — 🌅 Opening Activity: The Odds and Ends Hunt
- **9:00–9:15 AM** — Morning Meeting (15 min)
- **9:15–10:15 AM** — 🎨 The Main Event: The Forest Floor Tray (60 min)
- **10:15–10:30 AM** — Snack (15 min)
- **10:30–11:15 AM** — 🌳 Get Outside: What's Under the Leaves (45 min)
- **11:15–11:45 AM** — Free Play (30 min)
- **11:45 AM–12:00 PM** — 🤸 Wiggle Time (15 min)
- **12:00–12:30 PM** — Lunch
- **12:30–1:00 PM** — 📚 Book Time
- **1:00–2:30 PM** — 🎨 Second Main Event: Moving the Animals In (90 min)
- **2:30–3:30 PM** — 🌳 Out Again: **Pick one** (60 min)
- **3:30–4:00 PM** — Snack & Free Play
- **4:00–4:15 PM** — 🧹 Her Job: The Forks
- **4:15–5:00 PM** — Quiet Play: Rearranging the Tray
- **5:00–5:45 PM** — 🛁 Wind-Down: The Long Bath
- **6:00 PM** — **Dinner**

### 🌅 Opening Activity: The Odds and Ends Hunt

**🧰 You need:** the bowl of odds and ends · 1 baking tray · 1 jar lid.

1. Tip the bowl into the middle of the table and let her go through it.
2. Give her three words: **log**, **rock**, **leaf**. Ask which things in the pile could be each. A cork can be a log, a bottle top a rock.
3. Set the jar lid in the middle of the empty tray and tell her it is the pond. Nothing else goes in until 9:15.

### 🎨 The Main Event: The Forest Floor Tray

**🧰 Complete Materials List:**
- 1 baking tray or a large shallow box lid
- Soil, sand or torn brown paper for the ground
- Corks, clothes pegs, bottle tops, cotton wool, fabric scraps
- 1 jar lid for the pond
- Small stones and twigs from the garden
- 1 jug of water

**📝 Step-by-Step Instructions:**
1. Spread the ground first: soil, sand, or brown paper torn small. Her hands do it. Sweep at the end.
2. Press the jar lid in and let her pour water from the jug. Refill it rather than telling her to go careful.
3. Lay the corks as fallen logs and the pegs as branches. Stand the twigs up as trees, pushed in or taped to the tray edge.
4. Build one hollow under a cork to sleep in, and one heap of cotton wool for a nest.
5. Ring the pond with the small stones. Ask what is missing and put in whatever she names.
6. Leave one corner bare. You collect for it at 10:30 and fill it after lunch, so tell her that now.

> 💡 **Tip:** Give her the whole bowl, not a chosen handful. Deciding which cork is a log is the part she came for.

### 🌳 Get Outside: What's Under the Leaves

Take a paper bag and turn over the leaf litter at the bottom of a hedge. The
underneath of January is wet, dark and busy: leaves gone to skeleton, a
woodlouse, an empty snail shell, twigs going soft. Lift one stone, look, and
put it back as you found it. Fill the bag with moss, bark and four good twigs.

> 👶 **Infant Integration:** Kreston is 7 months and everything goes to his
> mouth. Keep him clear of the tray and give him a bowl of his own with a
> wooden spoon and a folded flannel in it. On the walk, hold one big dry leaf
> out for him to grab. He gets it to his mouth in under a second, so keep hold
> of your half.

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

### 🎨 Second Main Event: Moving the Animals In

The collecting bag has sat by the front door since eleven. Empty it into the
bare corner of the tray, and the Forest Family moves in on top.

**🧰 Complete Materials List:**
- The forest floor tray
- The collecting bag from this morning
- The Forest Family animals, or any toy animals
- 1 roll masking tape
- 1 spray bottle, or a spoon and a cup of water, for rain
- 1 permanent marker for name labels

**📝 Step-by-Step Instructions:**
1. Tip the bag onto newspaper beside the tray and go through it piece by piece. She decides what goes in.
2. Press the moss down as ground cover and lean the bark against a cork for a roof.
3. Bring the animals over one at a time. Each one needs somewhere to sleep and she picks the spot.
4. Write each name on masking tape and stick it on the tray edge below that animal.
5. Make it rain, one squeeze of the spray bottle or a spoonful tipped from a height. Watch where it runs and who chose a bad spot.
6. Move whoever got wet. Ask why the new place is drier and take her reasoning as it comes.
7. Lift the tray onto the table when she has finished. It comes down again tomorrow.

> 💡 **Tip:** Rain is the best thing here and she will ask for it eleven times. Set the number before you start, three rains, and hold the line, or the tray is soup by two.

### 🎯 Afternoon Alternatives
- A shoebox lid and four things, and stop there.
- Draw the forest floor on paper instead of building it.
- Sort the collecting bag into two piles, alive and not alive.
- Float the bark in the washing-up bowl and see what else floats.

### 🌳 Out Again: **Pick one**
- **A nature hunt walk** with a list of five: something red, rough, round, soft, and older than her
- The same hedge again with a torch as the light goes
- A woodland path, for trees she has not met yet
- The duck pond, to see what the water does in January
- The garden, re-hunted with a magnifying glass, if the weather has turned

> 👶 **Kreston's Afternoon:** He comes on the hunt in the carrier facing out
> with a hat over his ears. Stop three times and hold him up to touch a wall, a
> wet leaf, a cold gate. Name each one as his hand lands. At 7 months he answers
> every name with a run of sound, so leave him the gap to put it in.

---

> 🧠 **A Little Parenting Insight**
> **Loose parts beat finished toys.**
> A bowl of corks, pegs and bottle tops puts a decision in front of Azlyn on
> every piece she picks up. The cork has to become a log, and she makes it one.
> Architects and playworkers call these loose parts: the more a material can
> turn into, the longer a child stays with it and the more she says. A toy fox
> stays a fox for as long as you own it. Her cork is a log, then a seat, then a
> bear, and each change is a thought she had. Keep a bowl of them where she can
> reach and top it up from the recycling.

> ⚠️ **Safety:** Small stones, corks and bottle tops sit at mouth height for a
> 7-month-old. The tray lives on the table between sessions and never on the
> floor while Kreston is loose in the room. Soil and leaf litter mean hands
> washed before the snack. Tip the pond out when she has finished.

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

<a id="day-32"></a>

## 🌟 Day 32: Who Came Past in the Night 🌟
**📅 Monday, February 1, 2027**
**Theme:** Forest Friends

### 🌙 Prep Tonight
> Lay the flour trail tonight in the room you can shut the door on, hide the animal at the end, and leave the paw stamps and the sieve on the table.

### ⏰ At-a-Glance Schedule
- **8:00–9:00 AM** — 🌅 Opening Activity: The Four Feet
- **9:00–9:15 AM** — Morning Meeting (15 min)
- **9:15–10:15 AM** — 🎨 The Main Event: The Track Trail (60 min)
- **10:15–10:30 AM** — Snack (15 min)
- **10:30–11:15 AM** — 🌳 Get Outside: Footprints in the Frost (45 min)
- **11:15–11:45 AM** — Free Play (30 min)
- **11:45 AM–12:00 PM** — 🤸 Wiggle Time (15 min)
- **12:00–12:30 PM** — Lunch
- **12:30–1:00 PM** — 📚 Book Time
- **1:00–2:30 PM** — 🎨 Second Main Event: Laying a Trail for Joseph (90 min)
- **2:30–3:30 PM** — 🌳 Out Again: **Pick one** (60 min)
- **3:30–4:00 PM** — Snack & Free Play
- **4:00–4:15 PM** — 🧹 Her Job: The Forks
- **4:15–5:00 PM** — Quiet Play: The Print Book
- **5:00–5:45 PM** — 🛁 Wind-Down: Books on the Floor Cushions
- **6:00 PM** — **Dinner**

### 🌅 Opening Activity: The Four Feet

**🧰 You need:** paper · 1 crayon · the 2 paw stamps · one shut door.

1. Draw four feet on one sheet: a bird's, a duck's, a grown-up's, and a paw with four toes. Draw them rough; she will still read them.
2. Ask whose is whose and take the wrong answers without fixing them. Then ask what each foot is good at. Gripping, swimming, walking, digging.
3. Press one paw stamp onto the paper dry so she meets the shape before the trail. Something came through the house in the night, and that door stays shut until 9:15.

### 🎨 The Main Event: The Track Trail

**🧰 Complete Materials List:**
- Plain flour, 2 cups, in a sieve or a small bowl
- 2 paw shapes cut from a sponge or thick card
- Dark paper or a dark towel for the prints to show up on
- 3 clues: a chewed leaf, a tuft of wool, a few crumbs
- 1 hidden animal from the Forest Family, or any toy animal
- 1 dustpan and brush

**📝 Step-by-Step Instructions:**
1. Open the door and stand back. Let her look before anybody says a word.
2. Follow it from the start on hands and knees. Count the prints out loud and let her take over.
3. Stop at every clue and ask the same question: what does this tell us. A chewed leaf says it eats plants, a tuft of wool says it is furry, crumbs say it went past the bin.
4. Find the animal at the end. She lifts whatever it is hiding under and says the name.
5. Now walk it backwards, animal to door, and ask which way it was going. This is the hard part.
6. Sweep up together. Keep half a cup of flour back for after lunch.

> 💡 **Tip:** Do not tell her who it was at the start. Guessing wrong for ten minutes and finding out at the end is the whole activity.

### 🌳 Get Outside: Footprints in the Frost

Early February holds frost on grass and car roofs until the sun comes round.
Walk the shaded side of the street and read what crossed it in the
night: a cat's prints along a wall, bird feet in a frozen puddle, her own boots
on white grass. Send her across a frosted lawn, then turn her round to see
what she left.

> 👶 **Infant Integration:** Kreston is 7 months and sits solid with both hands
> free. Put him at the far end of the trail on a blanket with a wooden spoon
> and let Azlyn crawl toward him. He tracks her the whole way and laughs before
> she arrives. Keep him off the floured floor, and out in the frost hold his
> bare palm on a cold car roof for one second.

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

### 🎨 Second Main Event: Laying a Trail for Joseph

Joseph walks in at six tonight with no idea there is anything on the floor.
Azlyn spends the afternoon making sure there is.

**🧰 Complete Materials List:**
- The paw stamps and the flour
- Paper and crayons for arrow cards
- 1 roll masking tape
- One thing to hide at the end, chosen by her
- 1 torch
- 1 dustpan and brush

**📝 Step-by-Step Instructions:**
1. Walk the route with her first, no flour, and let her fix where it starts and ends. Follow her decision even when it goes through the kitchen twice.
2. She chooses what waits at the end and hides it herself, out of your sight.
3. Print the prints. She presses the stamp, you sieve a little flour onto the sponge between presses.
4. Make three arrow cards. She draws them, you tape them at Joseph's eye height rather than hers.
5. Add two clues of her own. Ask what each is meant to tell him and write her answer on the back.
6. Walk it once as a test, her leading and you playing Joseph. She will fix two things.
7. Turn the lights down at six and hand him the torch at the door.

> 💡 **Tip:** Write her clues down word for word. She will not remember the reasoning by Friday.

### 🎯 Afternoon Alternatives
- One arrow, one hidden thing, thirty seconds of trail.
- Lay the trail outside in chalk instead of flour.
- Draw round everybody's shoes and match them back to feet.
- Press a toy animal's feet into playdough.

### 🌳 Out Again: **Pick one**
- **The big slide playground**, and check under the swings for tracks
- The neighbourhood park, for the muddy path along the far end
- A field walk, for the biggest frozen puddles you can find
- The woods loop, looking for holes in banks and under roots
- Another trail upstairs, if the wind has made outside a bad idea

> 👶 **Kreston's Afternoon:** He goes in the pushchair with a blanket over his
> legs and a hat over his ears. February wind at the top of a slide is a
> different thing at his height, so keep him low and moving. Five minutes on
> the baby swing and no more. He is passing things hand to hand now, so give
> him two rings for the walk home.

---

> 🧠 **A Little Parenting Insight**
> **Evidence and inference, a big idea learnable at three.**
> A chewed leaf on the floor and a fox at the end of the hallway are different
> kinds of thing, and Azlyn spent the morning getting from one to the other.
> Scientists call the move inference: the leaf is what you can see, the fox is
> what you worked out. Three-year-olds do this better than the age suggests, as
> long as the evidence sits in front of them. Ask *what does this tell us*
> instead of *who was it*, and she has to say the middle step out loud. That
> middle step is the whole of science, and she will use it on you within a week.

> ⚠️ **Safety:** Flour on a hard floor is slippery in a way that puts adults
> down as fast as children. Sweep between rounds, keep it off tile and
> laminate near the stairs, and never lay a trail on a staircase. Sieve it
> rather than throwing it, so nobody breathes a cloud.

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

<a id="day-33"></a>

## 🌟 Day 33: Feeding the Birds 🌟
**📅 Tuesday, February 2, 2027**
**Theme:** Forest Friends

### 🌙 Prep Tonight
> Take the fat out of the fridge, wash four yoghurt pots, and stand the seed, the string and the swimming bag by the door.

### ⏰ At-a-Glance Schedule
- **8:00–9:00 AM** — 🌅 Opening Activity: The Bird Menu
- **9:00–9:15 AM** — Morning Meeting (15 min)
- **9:15–10:15 AM** — 🎨 The Main Event: The Bird Café (60 min)
- **10:15–10:30 AM** — Snack (15 min)
- **10:30–11:15 AM** — 🌳 Get Outside: Hanging the Feeders (45 min)
- **11:15–11:45 AM** — Free Play (30 min)
- **11:45 AM–12:00 PM** — 🤸 Wiggle Time (15 min)
- **12:00–12:30 PM** — Lunch
- **12:30–1:00 PM** — 📚 Book Time
- **1:00–2:30 PM** — 🎨 Second Main Event: Binoculars and the Bird Chart (90 min)
- **2:30–3:30 PM** — 🌳 Out Again: **Swimming** (60 min)
- **3:30–4:00 PM** — Snack & Free Play
- **4:00–4:15 PM** — 🧹 Her Job: Topping Up the Seed Tub
- **4:15–5:00 PM** — Quiet Play: The Window Seat
- **5:00–5:45 PM** — 🛁 Wind-Down: After Swimming, Two Books
- **6:00 PM** — **Dinner**

### 🌅 Opening Activity: The Bird Menu

**🧰 You need:** paper · crayons · a saucer of seed, oats and raisins.

1. Put the saucer in front of her and let her sort it. Seeds here, oats there, raisins in their own heap.
2. Ask which a bird would go for first. Write her answer as a menu, one line per item, with her drawing of the bird beside it.
3. Pin the menu on the window the feeders hang outside. She checks it against real birds all week.

### 🎨 The Main Event: The Bird Café

**🧰 Complete Materials List:**
- Solid vegetable fat or lard, 250 g (peanut butter works if nobody in the house has a nut allergy)
- Birdseed, or porridge oats plus breadcrumbs and raisins, 3 cups
- Empty yoghurt pots, 4, or pine cones
- String, 1 ball
- 1 mixing bowl and 1 wooden spoon
- 1 skewer or scissors for the holes (**adult**)

**📝 Step-by-Step Instructions:**
1. Make the holes first, while your hands are clean. Push the skewer through the base of each pot, thread string through and knot it inside.
2. Warm the fat to soft, well short of clear, and let it stand until you can hold a finger in it.
3. Tip the seed into the bowl, add the fat, and let her work them together with a spoon and then both hands. Let this part run long.
4. Pack the mix into the pots and press it down hard with her thumbs, up to the rim.
5. Roll a pine cone in what is left and tie a string round the top.
6. Stand them in the fridge while you have snack. They go outside at 10:30, and any still soft go out when you get back from swimming.

> 💡 **Tip:** Cool the fat right down, then let her hands live in that bowl as long as she wants.

### 🌳 Get Outside: Hanging the Feeders

Hang all four where she can see them from a window and a cat cannot reach. A
washing line, a bare branch, a hook under the eaves, a fence post. She picks
every spot and you lift her to the high ones. Count from inside how many she
can see. February is the hard month out there, and this one gets found inside
a week.

> 👶 **Infant Integration:** Kreston is 7 months and passes things from hand to
> hand all day. Give him a clean yoghurt pot and a wooden spoon beside the
> table and let him swap them over. He bangs the two together inside ten
> minutes. The seed, the string and the fat stay well out of reach.

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

### 🎨 Second Main Event: Binoculars and the Bird Chart

Nothing will come to those feeders today. Birds take a day or two to find a
new one, so build her somewhere to sit and wait.

**🧰 Complete Materials List:**
- Cardboard tubes, 4
- 1 roll masking tape
- Paper and crayons for the chart
- Cushions and a blanket for the window seat
- 1 pencil
- The Groundhog Day note: February 2, when a woodchuck is said to come up and check on winter

**📝 Step-by-Step Instructions:**
1. Tape two tubes side by side for her pair, and two more for yours. Nothing goes round her neck: she holds hers to her eyes with both hands.
2. Let her colour both pairs.
3. Build the window seat: cushions under the window, blanket over her knees, chart and pencil beside her.
4. Draw the chart with her. Five rows, one bird per row, a tick box at the end.
5. Sit and watch. Ten minutes is a long time at three, so set the deal now: watch, something else, watch again.
6. Tick nothing, and treat that as an answer. Ask why and write her reason at the bottom.
7. Tell her what today is. February 2, Groundhog Day. Ask what she thinks the woodchuck saw.

> 💡 **Tip:** Leave the chart on the windowsill with the pencil. She ticks it on Thursday when a blue tit turns up.

### 🎯 Afternoon Alternatives
- One tube, one eye, and stop there.
- Draw birds out of a book instead.
- Count everything that moves outside for five minutes.
- Put crumbs on the windowsill and watch the sill instead.

### 🌳 Out Again: **Swimming**
- **Swimming**, the weekly one
- The quiet hour, if the after-school crowd is in
- Soft play if the pool is shut
- A warm café and the new binoculars, aimed at the pigeons
- Straight home, if the morning ran long

> 👶 **Kreston's Afternoon:** He swims too if he is coming. At 7 months he sits
> supported in the water and slaps at it, and twenty minutes is enough before
> he goes cold. Hold him chest to chest for the first few minutes, so the echo
> and the shouting arrive with your face in front of him. Get him dry and
> dressed before you deal with yourself.

---

> 🧠 **A Little Parenting Insight**
> **Delayed payoff is a muscle, and this is a gentle way to work it.**
> She packed four feeders this morning and no bird came this afternoon. That
> gap is the thing worth having. Waiting well builds through small waits with
> something visible at the end, and a feeder outside the window is the
> friendliest version. Nobody is asking her to sit still in front of a
> marshmallow. She hung it, she can see it, and the birds arrive on Thursday.
> Say out loud that they need a day or two to find it, so the wait carries a
> reason instead of an unexplained no. A three-year-old who waited and turned
> out right takes that with her.

> ⚠️ **Safety:** Melt the fat to soft and never to liquid, and let it cool
> until you can hold a finger in it before her hands go near. Whole
> nuts are a choking hazard, and peanut butter is out if there is any nut
> allergy in the house. The string gets tied outside and the ball
> comes straight back indoors, because a loop of string at toddler height is a
> strangulation risk. The binoculars stay stringless for the same reason: no
> cord long enough to go over her head, on those or on anything else in the
> house. The skewer stays with you.

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

<a id="day-34"></a>

## 🌟 Day 34: The Long Sleep 🌟
**📅 Wednesday, February 3, 2027**
**Theme:** Forest Friends

### 🌙 Prep Tonight
> Empty the biggest box in the house, check inside for staples, and stand it on its side in the corner with a folded blanket beside it.

### ⏰ At-a-Glance Schedule
- **8:00–9:00 AM** — 🌅 Opening Activity: The Winter Sleepers
- **9:00–9:15 AM** — Morning Meeting (15 min)
- **9:15–10:15 AM** — 🎨 The Main Event: The Bear Cave (60 min)
- **10:15–10:30 AM** — Snack (15 min)
- **10:30–11:15 AM** — 🌳 Get Outside: Empty Nests in Bare Trees (45 min)
- **11:15–11:45 AM** — Free Play (30 min)
- **11:45 AM–12:00 PM** — 🤸 Wiggle Time (15 min)
- **12:00–12:30 PM** — Lunch
- **12:30–1:00 PM** — 📚 Book Time
- **1:00–2:30 PM** — 🎨 Second Main Event: Waking the Bears (90 min)
- **2:30–3:30 PM** — 🌳 Out Again: **The Library** (60 min)
- **3:30–4:00 PM** — Snack & Free Play
- **4:00–4:15 PM** — 🧹 Her Job: The Forks and the Napkins
- **4:15–5:00 PM** — Quiet Play: Nesting the Animals
- **5:00–5:45 PM** — 🛁 Wind-Down: The Library Book in the Cave
- **6:00 PM** — **Dinner**

### 🌅 Opening Activity: The Winter Sleepers

**🧰 You need:** the Forest Family animals or any toys · 1 blanket · the sofa.

1. Line the animals along the arm of the sofa. Ask which of them she thinks sleeps the whole winter.
2. Hand her the four that do in this house's version: bear, hedgehog, dormouse, bat. Tuck them under a corner of the blanket together.
3. Ask what a sleeping animal needs. Take her list as it comes, and tell her the cave gets built at 9:15.

### 🎨 The Main Event: The Bear Cave

**🧰 Complete Materials List:**
- 1 large cardboard box, big enough for her to sit up in
- Newspaper or scrap paper to tear for leaves
- 1 blanket and 1 cushion
- 1 roll masking tape
- 1 torch
- The Forest Family animals, or any toy animals

**📝 Step-by-Step Instructions:**
1. Lay the box on its side with the opening facing away from the window. Run a hand round the inside for staples and tape over the ones you find.
2. Tear the paper into leaves together. Fill a carrier bag and let the tearing run as long as she wants.
3. Line the floor of the cave with the leaves, the blanket over them, and the cushion at the back wall.
4. Tape a flap of card across part of the opening so the inside goes dark. She decides how much light gets in.
5. Send her in with the torch and stay outside. Give her five clear minutes on her own before you ask a single question.
6. Move the animals in one at a time, each to a spot she picks. Then get in with her, turn the torch off, and let the cave go dark.

> 💡 **Tip:** Let her tear far more paper than the cave needs. Two hands pulling in opposite directions is work she can keep up for twenty minutes.

### 🌳 Get Outside: Empty Nests in Bare Trees

Nests show up in February and vanish again in April, so this is the month to
look. Walk the road and the park with your eyes up and count the dark clumps
in the bare branches. Some are nests, some are a knot of twigs. Find the
highest and the lowest. Nobody is home in any of them, so say so.

> 👶 **Infant Integration:** Kreston is 7 months. Sit him at the cave mouth
> propped on a cushion with you beside him, and let Azlyn crawl in and out past
> him. The light drops as she goes in and comes back as she comes out, and he
> turns his head after it. Give him a big sheet of paper to crush in both hands.

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

### 🎨 Second Main Event: Waking the Bears

The cave is dark and lined by lunchtime. Azlyn runs the afternoon on a wooden
spoon and a pan lid, and winter ends whenever she says it does.

**🧰 Complete Materials List:**
- The cave
- 1 wooden spoon and 1 pan lid
- 1 blanket each
- 1 beaker of warm milk and a snack, for eating inside
- Books about bears or winter, 2–3
- 1 torch

**📝 Step-by-Step Instructions:**
1. Everybody hibernates. Into the cave or under a blanket, eyes shut, and hold it while you count to twenty out loud.
2. Bang the pan lid once with the spoon. That is spring. Everybody wakes, stretches and comes out slow.
3. Go round again with her holding the spoon. She decides how long winter lasts, three seconds the first time and two minutes the fourth.
4. Change one rule each round. Sleep standing up. Sleep in a pile. Sleep with the torch on. Sleep and snore.
5. Creep past a sleeping bear without waking it, then swap and let her creep past you. Getting caught is the fun of it, so get caught.
6. Take the milk and the snack into the cave and eat in there with the torch on.
7. Read the books in the cave and finish with everybody asleep and nobody banging anything.

> 💡 **Tip:** Let her hold the pan lid for most of the rounds. Deciding when winter ends is a lot of power for a three-year-old.

### 🎯 Afternoon Alternatives
- A blanket over two sofa cushions instead of a box.
- Sleeping lions, the quiet version, five minutes and done.
- Draw what a bear dreams about all winter.
- Sort the animals into sleepers and stay-awakes.

### 🌳 Out Again: **The Library**
- **The library**, and ask at the desk for one book about bears and one about winter
- Soft play if the library is shut or heaving
- The park on the way back, for twenty minutes of running
- A café, with both new books read cover to cover
- The bookshop window, if the library run is not happening today

> 👶 **Kreston's Afternoon:** Sit him in the board-book corner at the library
> while Azlyn chooses. At 7 months he mouths every book he gets hold of, and he
> holds one in each hand and swaps them. Let Azlyn pick his and carry it to the
> desk. At home the pan lid stays well away from his ears.

---

> 🧠 **A Little Parenting Insight**
> **Pretending to sleep is real self-regulation practice.**
> Azlyn lying still with her eyes shut while you count to twenty is doing
> something hard on purpose. She wants to move, she knows the rule, and she
> holds herself to it anyway. Researchers call that inhibitory control, and
> games with a stop-and-go rule build it better than being told to sit still,
> because the rule belongs to the game. The round where she holds the pan lid
> works harder: running the stop and the go for somebody else takes more
> control than following it. Freeze games, musical statues and sleeping lions
> sit in the same family. Reach for one on the afternoons she is climbing the
> walls.

> ⚠️ **Safety:** Kreston is 7 months and cannot get himself out of anywhere.
> He goes in the cave only with you in it, and never on his own for a second.
> Torn paper and loose blankets stay clear of his face. Check the box for
> staples before either of them climbs in, and tape over every one you find.

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

<a id="day-35"></a>

## 🌟 Day 35: The Shadow Forest 🌟
**📅 Thursday, February 4, 2027**
**Theme:** Forest Friends

### 🌙 Prep Tonight
> Flatten two cereal boxes onto the table with the scissors and a marker, put fresh batteries in the torch, and clear the wall.

### ⏰ At-a-Glance Schedule
- **8:00–9:00 AM** — 🌅 Opening Activity: Guess the Shadow
- **9:00–9:15 AM** — Morning Meeting (15 min)
- **9:15–10:15 AM** — 🎨 The Main Event: The Shadow Animals (60 min)
- **10:15–10:30 AM** — Snack (15 min)
- **10:30–11:15 AM** — 🌳 Get Outside: The Longest Shadow (45 min)
- **11:15–11:45 AM** — Free Play (30 min)
- **11:45 AM–12:00 PM** — 🤸 Wiggle Time (15 min)
- **12:00–12:30 PM** — Lunch
- **12:30–1:00 PM** — 📚 Book Time
- **1:00–2:30 PM** — 🎨 Second Main Event: The Puppet Show Behind the Sheet (90 min)
- **2:30–3:30 PM** — 🌳 Out Again: **Pick one** (60 min)
- **3:30–4:00 PM** — Snack & Free Play
- **4:00–4:15 PM** — 🧹 Her Job: The Forks
- **4:15–5:00 PM** — Quiet Play: The Torch and the Wall
- **5:00–5:45 PM** — 🛁 Wind-Down: Last Story of the Week
- **6:00 PM** — **Dinner**

### 🌅 Opening Activity: Guess the Shadow

**🧰 You need:** 1 torch · a pale wall · 3 toy animals · your hands.

1. Turn the big light off, sit her facing the wall, and hold the torch behind your shoulder.
2. Put one animal in the beam. Ask which one it is before she turns round.
3. Move it closer to the torch and let the shadow swell. Hand her the torch until 9:15.

### 🎨 The Main Event: The Shadow Animals

**🧰 Complete Materials List:**
- Cereal boxes or thick card, 4–5
- Scissors (**adult**)
- Lolly sticks, straws or pencils
- 1 roll masking tape
- 1 torch
- A pale wall or a white door

**📝 Step-by-Step Instructions:**
1. Draw the four animals on the cereal box: fox, owl, deer, hedgehog. Big simple outlines, no detail inside. You draw, she directs.
2. Cut them out. Card this thick is adult work: she holds the box flat while you cut.
3. Tape a lolly stick low on the back of each, so her hand stays out of the beam.
4. Test the first one against the wall. If the ears are too small or the legs have joined into a block, cut wider and test again.
5. Do the same to all four. Deer antlers have to be fat, and an owl needs a notch between its ear tufts.
6. Line them up and test from across the room. Any animal she cannot name goes back for cutting.

> 💡 **Tip:** Cut everything fatter than looks right. Thin legs and fine antlers vanish on the wall.

### 🌳 Get Outside: The Longest Shadow

Stop at the feeders on the way out. Something has been at the fat, and a blue
tit comes back to finish it if you hold still. Let her tick
the chart when you get in. The sun stays low all day in early February, so
shadows run long. Stand her on the pavement at eleven, draw round her shadow
in chalk, and pace it out in her steps. Come back past the chalk mark on the
way home.

> 👶 **Infant Integration:** Kreston is 7 months. Lay him on his back and hold
> the torch above him off to one side, your hand between the beam and his face.
> Move your fingers through it. He follows the shape across the ceiling,
> loses it, finds it again. Outside, park him with the low sun behind him and
> wave from a few feet back.

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

### 🎨 Second Main Event: The Puppet Show Behind the Sheet

A sheet pegged across a doorway turns the week into something she can
perform. She has four animals, one torch, and an audience.

**🧰 Complete Materials List:**
- 1 white or pale sheet
- Clothes pegs or bulldog clips, 6
- A doorway, or 2 dining chairs back to back
- 1 torch
- The shadow puppets
- Paper and crayons for tickets and a poster

**📝 Step-by-Step Instructions:**
1. Peg the sheet across the doorway, pulled tight, floor to above her head. Two chairs back to back work if clips will not hold.
2. Stand the torch behind the sheet on the floor or a low stool, aimed at the middle.
3. Test the distance. Close to the sheet the fox is small and sharp; back near the torch he is huge and soft.
4. Make the poster and the tickets. She draws, you write the words she dictates.
5. Sit the audience down: Kreston propped on cushions, teddies either side, whoever else is home.
6. She tells the week back through the puppets: coats on Friday, the tree on Saturday, somebody past in the night on Monday. Give her the day and she supplies the rest.
7. Swap sides at the end. You run the fox, and the last five minutes go with the torch off.

> 💡 **Tip:** Move the torch back further than feels right. A torch against the sheet gives one bright spot and four tiny animals.

### 🎯 Afternoon Alternatives
- Torch and bare hands on the wall, no sheet and no puppets.
- Shadow shapes on the ceiling from under a blanket.
- Draw round the puppets' shadows on paper and colour them in.
- Retire the puppets into the forest floor tray and stop there.

### 🌳 Out Again: **Pick one**
- **The garden centre**, for the indoor trees and a warm greenhouse in February
- The hardware shop, for torches and a spare set of batteries
- The park on the way back, for the low sun and the long shadows
- The grocery shop, and she chooses one thing for Friday breakfast
- Home and the sofa, if the show ran long and nobody wants a coat on

> 👶 **Kreston's Afternoon:** The garden centre is warm and bright, so sit him
> on your lap for ten minutes. Hold a big leaf out to one side, where he
> has to reach across himself. At 7 months that cross-body reach is new work.
> At the show he sits in the front row, and moving shapes on a lit sheet hold
> him longer than a book.

---

> 🧠 **A Little Parenting Insight**
> **Shadows are confusing at three, and the confusion is worth exploring.**
> Ask Azlyn what a shadow is made of and you get real thought in the answer and
> no truth in it. Three-year-olds treat a shadow as a thing that belongs to an
> object and travels with it, rather than as blocked light. The real version
> takes a couple of years of torch time. Every test today feeds that
> version: close to the sheet, back near the torch, one hand in the beam. Hold
> off on the tidy explanation. Give her the torch, let her run the
> tests, and answer the questions she asks rather than the ones she has not
> reached.

> ⚠️ **Safety:** Loose lolly sticks and straws stay off the floor while Kreston
> is down. They splinter, and they fit down his throat, so count them onto the
> table and back off it. Card this thick is yours to cut and the scissors stay
> with you. Peg the sheet where it can come down without bringing anything with
> it, a door frame rather than a curtain pole or a shelf. Stand the torch on
> the floor instead of a stack of books, and with the room lights off keep
> Kreston inside arm's reach the whole time.

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
<a id="week-6"></a>

## Week 6: Little Bakery 🥐
**📅 February 5 – February 11, 2027**

---

### 🎯 What This Week Is About

Four things make bread, and she counts them on her fingers on Friday morning
before anything goes in the bowl: flour, water, salt, yeast. The rest of the
week takes them apart one at a time. The yeast gets its own jar and then its
own balloon, the dough gets punched flat and stretched until it tears, and the
same lump comes back as rolls, a pizza base and a tray of cinnamon buns. She
follows one loop all week, from dry powder foaming in warm water to a hollow
knock on the bottom of a crust.

No holiday lands in this week, so the shape comes from the theme. Four days
run on a hot oven, three of them on real dough. Monday and Wednesday run on playdough, paper
and a pretend shop counter, and they sit there to give your kitchen a day off.
Thursday takes the whole thing out of the house: she boxes what she baked,
walks a round she scouted that morning, and hands it over at four front doors.

---

### 🧒 What Azlyn Will Learn

- **Yeast is alive and it eats.** A jar of foam at half eight, a balloon standing up by half eleven, and an hour of waiting she can see the point of.
- **Hands that knead, roll, cut and press.** Ten minutes of heavy work at the table on Friday, a rolling pin on Monday, and floss pulled through a log of dough on Thursday.
- **Her own words hold still on paper.** She dictates a recipe, you read it back the same way twice, and by Thursday she picks *butter* out of the page on her own.
- **Words for what her mouth finds.** Soft, chewy, seedy, holey. Five breads on five numbered plates and one word each.
- **Numbers doing a job.** Twelve pastries counted onto a tray, a price written on every one, buttons counted into the till and back out at closing.

---

### 👩 What You'll Learn

- Auditory attention is trainable, and it underpins reading
- Dictated stories are the bridge to real literacy
- Cabin fever is a real physiological thing, not a bad attitude
- Pretend play is where self-control gets built
- Regression during illness is expected, not a setback
- Boredom on the mend is a good sign, and a useful one
- On the days you use this page instead of the plan

---

### 🛒 This Week's Shopping List

> *Everything both main events call for, all seven days. Check what you've
> already got before Friday.*

- [ ] Strong white flour, 1.5 kg
- [ ] Plain flour, 1 kg
- [ ] Salt, 500 g (a full cup of it goes into Monday's playdough)
- [ ] Sugar, 250 g
- [ ] Dried yeast, 6 sachets of 7 g
- [ ] Butter, 250 g (100 g soft for Saturday, 75 g soft for Thursday)
- [ ] Vegetable oil
- [ ] 1 egg
- [ ] Double cream, 300 ml
- [ ] Milk
- [ ] Ground cinnamon
- [ ] Dried herbs
- [ ] Her chosen additions: raisins, chocolate chips, cocoa, sprinkles or a banana
- [ ] Passata, or a tin of chopped tomatoes
- [ ] Grated cheese, 200 g
- [ ] Pizza toppings from the fridge: ham, sweetcorn, peppers, olives, mushrooms
- [ ] Five breads for Wednesday: sliced white, a seeded roll, a bagel, a pita, a crumpet
- [ ] Crackers
- [ ] Food colouring, 2–3 colours
- [ ] Two clean jars, one of them with a tight-fitting lid
- [ ] Clear plastic bottles, 2 × 500 ml, from the recycling
- [ ] Balloons, 4 (two for the bottles, two spare)
- [ ] Baking trays, 3
- [ ] A round tin
- [ ] An old baking tray and a plastic tub for the mud bakery
- [ ] Baking paper
- [ ] Paper cake cases
- [ ] A wire rack, or a cold plate
- [ ] Oven gloves
- [ ] A rolling pin
- [ ] Mixing bowls, 1 large and 2 small
- [ ] A sieve
- [ ] A wooden spoon, a teaspoon, a serving spoon per person, a fork and a pastry brush
- [ ] A table knife and a butter knife
- [ ] A big board for cutting the pizzas on
- [ ] A bread knife (adult only)
- [ ] Scissors (adult only)
- [ ] Dental floss
- [ ] A funnel, or paper to roll into a cone
- [ ] A jug, 2 mugs and 5 plates
- [ ] Two tea towels
- [ ] A stool she can stand on
- [ ] Plain paper, 25 sheets
- [ ] Crayons or felt tips
- [ ] A permanent marker and a pen
- [ ] Stickers
- [ ] Sticky labels, 5
- [ ] Tape and glue
- [ ] String
- [ ] A hole punch, or a stapler
- [ ] A cereal box, flattened for a cover
- [ ] Paper bags or envelopes, 8
- [ ] Small boxes from the recycling, 4
- [ ] A shoebox for the till
- [ ] Buttons or bottle tops, 30, and a purse
- [ ] Fifty pence in coins for Thursday's charity shop

---

### 💛 A Note from Joseph

> *(Draft. Rewrite this. I can guess the shape of it, not the feeling.)*
>
> Brooklyn, the oven is on four days out of seven this week and Kreston picked
> February to start crossing floors. I know what those two cost when they land
> in the same room, and the plan already gives some of it back. Monday and
> Wednesday are playdough and paper on purpose, so if the bread days come down
> to a bought pack and a lot of knocking on crusts, take that and call it done.
> The one I would keep is Thursday. She walks the round in the morning with her
> hands empty, and at half two she carries a box up somebody's path and gets
> the words out herself while you stand back on the paving. I am at work for
> that. Tell me who answered the door and what she said.

---

> 👶 **Kreston is 8 months this week**. He crawls or shuffles, brings two
> objects together to bang them, and checks your face before he decides what a
> new thing is for. **He turns 8 months on Friday the 5th, the first day of the week,
> and stranger wariness arrives with it.**

---

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

<a id="day-36"></a>

## 🌟 Day 36: The Dough That Wakes Up 🌟
**📅 Friday, February 5, 2027**
**Theme:** Little Bakery

### 🌙 Prep Tonight
> Set the flour, salt, sugar, a sachet of dried yeast and a clean jam jar on the table, and stand 100 g of butter out for tomorrow.

### ⏰ At-a-Glance Schedule
- **8:00–9:00 AM** — 🌅 Opening Activity: The Fizzing Jar
- **9:00–9:15 AM** — Morning Meeting (15 min)
- **9:15–10:15 AM** — 🎨 The Main Event: Four Things Make Bread (60 min)
- **10:15–10:30 AM** — Snack (15 min)
- **10:30–11:15 AM** — 🌳 Get Outside: The Walk Past the Bakery (45 min)
- **11:15–11:45 AM** — Free Play (30 min)
- **11:45 AM–12:00 PM** — 🤸 Wiggle Time (15 min)
- **12:00–12:30 PM** — Lunch
- **12:30–1:00 PM** — 📚 Book Time
- **1:00–2:30 PM** — 🎨 Second Main Event: The Rolls and the Hollow Knock (90 min)
- **2:30–3:30 PM** — 🌳 Out Again: **Pick one** (60 min)
- **3:30–4:00 PM** — Snack & Free Play
- **4:00–4:15 PM** — 🧹 Her Job: The Bread Basket
- **4:15–5:00 PM** — Quiet Play: Her Own Piece of Dough
- **5:00–5:45 PM** — 🛁 Wind-Down: Bath and the Warm Kitchen
- **6:00 PM** — **Dinner**

### 🌅 Opening Activity: The Fizzing Jar

**🧰 You need:** 1 clean jam jar · dried yeast, 1 sachet · sugar, 1 tsp · warm water, 100 ml · 1 teaspoon.

1. Fill the jar a third full from the hot tap with cold run in until it feels like bath water on your wrist. Let her test it with one finger.
2. Tip in the yeast and the sugar and let her stir. Then walk away for ten minutes.
3. Come back and look. The top has gone beige and foamy and smells of beer. Tell her the yeast is alive and has been eating the sugar.

### 🎨 The Main Event: Four Things Make Bread

**🧰 Complete Materials List:**
- Strong white flour, 500 g
- Dried yeast, 1 sachet (7 g)
- Salt, 1 tsp
- Sugar, 1 tsp
- Warm water, 300 ml
- Vegetable oil, 1 tbsp
- 1 large mixing bowl
- 1 clean tea towel
- The jam jar of woken yeast from 8:00

**📝 Step-by-Step Instructions:**
1. Count the four things on her fingers before anything goes in the bowl: flour, water, salt, yeast.
2. Flour into the bowl, salt to one side, and let her punch a well in the middle. The yeast goes in the well, then the oil and the warm water.
3. She mixes with one hand until it comes together as a shaggy lump. It sticks to her. Leave it sticking.
4. Turn it onto the table and knead: push with the heel of your hand, fold, turn a quarter, push again. Say the four words out loud and hand it over.
5. Ten minutes of that between you. She will manage two and wander off, so take it on and hand it back when she returns. The lump goes from ragged to smooth.
6. Oil the bowl, drop the dough in, cover it and stand it somewhere warm. Mark its height on the outside with a strip of tape.

> 💡 **Tip:** Kneading is heavy work and it settles a wound-up morning better than any instruction to calm down. Give her all ten minutes.

### 🌳 Get Outside: The Walk Past the Bakery

Walk to whichever shop near you bakes on the premises. The supermarket counter
counts. Cold February air holds the smell tight to the doorway, so the last
four steps are the good ones. Count the shapes through the glass: rounds, long
ones, plaits, rolls with seeds. Buy one thing and let her carry it home in her
own hand.

> 👶 **Infant Integration:** **Kreston turns 8 months today** and he is
> crawling, which changes what a kitchen is. Set him up at the far end of the
> room with two metal spoons and a plastic bowl. Banging two objects together
> is new this month and needs both hands working to one plan. Say happy eight
> months out loud.

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

### 🎨 Second Main Event: The Rolls and the Hollow Knock

The dough has doubled by one o'clock and it holds the dent when she pushes a
finger in.

**🧰 Complete Materials List:**
- The risen dough
- 1 baking tray and baking paper
- Extra flour for the table
- 2 tbsp milk and a pastry brush or a spoon
- 1 wire rack or a cold plate
- Oven gloves
- Butter for the first one, eaten warm

**📝 Step-by-Step Instructions:**
1. Tip the dough onto a floured table and let her press all the air out of it. It sighs down and she will want to do it twice.
2. Cut it into eight or ten pieces. She cuts, you even them up afterwards without saying so.
3. Show her the roll: hand cupped over the piece, palm circling, dough turning underneath. Hers come out as lumps and lumps bake fine.
4. Space them on the lined tray a hand's width apart, cover, and leave them thirty minutes. Oven on to 220°C.
5. Brush the tops with milk and bake fifteen to eighteen minutes. She watches through the glass and the door stays shut.
6. Out onto the rack. Once one has cooled enough to hold, turn it over and knock the bottom with a knuckle. Hollow means baked through, so let her knock every one.
7. Butter the first and eat it standing up in the kitchen before anyone suggests plates.

> 💡 **Tip:** Knock on a raw one before the tray goes in. She needs the dull thud at half one to hear the hollow one an hour later.

### 🎯 Afternoon Alternatives
- A shop-bought pack of dough, and skip to the knocking.
- Shape the whole batch as one loaf and cut a cross in the top.
- Roll long snakes and plait three of them together.

### 🌳 Out Again: **Pick one**
- **A friend's house**, and she carries a bag of the rolls to the door herself
- The park, for the swings before the light goes
- The corner shop for butter and jam, both chosen by her
- A café, where somebody else has done the baking
- Back past the bakery window to see what has sold out

> 👶 **Kreston's Afternoon:** Kreston gets a soft roll with the crust off, torn
> small, sitting upright with you in front of him. Stranger wariness is
> arriving this month, so at the friend's house keep him on your hip for the
> first ten minutes. He comes off you when he leans off you and not before.

### 🌍 Around the World: Bread Everywhere 🥖

Almost everywhere on earth makes a bread from the same short list. India tears
**naan** off the wall of a clay oven. Mexico presses **tortillas** flat between
two hands. Ethiopia ferments **injera** until it turns sour, and it works as
the plate as well as the bread. France buys a **baguette** twice a day because
it goes stale by evening.

> **✨ Try it:** Buy one bread you have never eaten, tear it at the table and
> eat it with your hands. Azlyn decides whether it beats her rolls.

> *Did you know?* Injera is cooked on one side only, which is why the top is
> covered in tiny holes.

---

> 🧠 **A Little Parenting Insight**
> **Auditory attention is trainable, and it underpins reading.**
> Azlyn knocking the bottom of a roll and deciding whether it sounds hollow is
> doing fine listening work: two sounds held side by side, one picked out from
> the other. That skill sits underneath reading. Before she can match a letter
> to a sound she has to hear that *bat* and *pat* start with different mouths,
> and children who catch small differences in sound learn to read with less
> effort later on. Kitchens are good ground for it. The kettle, the fizzing
> jar, the knock on a crust, the oven timer. Name a sound out loud and she
> starts collecting them.

> ⚠️ **Safety:** Kreston is crawling and the oven runs at 220°C today, with hot
> trays coming out at his head height. Block his route to the oven door with a
> chair or the pram before the dough goes in, and check it is still there
> before you open the door. Never leave the oven door open to cool with him
> loose in the room.

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

## 🌟 Day 37: Azlyn's Own Recipe 🌟
**📅 Saturday, February 6, 2027**
**Theme:** Little Bakery

### 🌙 Prep Tonight
> Mix tomorrow's dough tonight (500 g strong flour, 7 g yeast, 1 tsp salt, 300 ml water, 1 tbsp oil), cover the bowl and leave it in the fridge.

### ⏰ At-a-Glance Schedule
- **8:00–9:00 AM** — 🌅 Opening Activity: Choosing Off the Shelf
- **9:00–9:15 AM** — Morning Meeting (15 min)
- **9:15–10:15 AM** — 🎨 The Main Event: The Recipe She Tells You (60 min)
- **10:15–10:30 AM** — Snack (15 min)
- **10:30–11:15 AM** — 🌳 Get Outside: The Frost Patch Hunt (45 min)
- **11:15–11:45 AM** — Free Play (30 min)
- **11:45 AM–12:00 PM** — 🤸 Wiggle Time (15 min)
- **12:00–12:30 PM** — Lunch
- **12:30–1:00 PM** — 📚 Book Time
- **1:00–2:30 PM** — 🎨 Second Main Event: Her Page in the Cookbook (90 min)
- **2:30–3:30 PM** — 🌳 Out Again: **The Museum** (60 min)
- **3:30–4:00 PM** — Snack & Free Play
- **4:00–4:15 PM** — 🧹 Her Job: The Forks
- **4:15–5:00 PM** — Quiet Play: The Cookbook Corner
- **5:00–5:45 PM** — 🛁 Wind-Down: Bath and Two Books
- **6:00 PM** — **Dinner**

### 🌅 Opening Activity: Choosing Off the Shelf

**🧰 You need:** the baking cupboard · a stool to stand on · paper and a pen.

1. Open the cupboard and get her up to eye level with the shelf. Read out what is in each tub and let her hold the ones with lids.
2. She picks the additions for her recipe. If that is sprinkles and mashed banana together, it is sprinkles and mashed banana together.
3. Write her choices at the top of a fresh page under her name. Nothing gets vetoed.

### 🎨 The Main Event: The Recipe She Tells You

**🧰 Complete Materials List:**
- Soft butter, 100 g
- Sugar, 100 g
- Plain flour, 200 g
- 1 egg
- Her chosen additions: raisins, chocolate chips, cocoa, sprinkles or a mashed banana
- 1 bowl and a wooden spoon
- 1 baking tray and baking paper
- Paper and a pen for her words

**📝 Step-by-Step Instructions:**
1. Sit down with her and ask how it is made, starting with the first thing into the bowl.
2. Write every word as it comes. *And then you shake it a lot.* *Two hundred sugars.* Do not tidy the order or fix the grammar.
3. Read it back from the top. She will change things. Cross out and write the new version underneath so both stay.
4. Now make it off her page. Quantities come from the list above, order comes from her.
5. Roll the mixture into walnut-sized balls, flatten them onto the lined tray with gaps between, and she adds her extras where her page says.
6. Bake at 180°C for twelve to fifteen minutes until the edges brown. Read her recipe out loud once more while they bake.

> 💡 **Tip:** Where her order makes a mess of it, follow it and say out loud what happened. A batch that comes out odd beats one you fixed behind her back.

### 🌳 Get Outside: The Frost Patch Hunt

Go out before the sun reaches the shady side of the street. Frost survives
where it has not: north-facing walls, the ground under parked cars, grass in
the shadow of a hedge. Give her five patches to find and a fingernail to
scratch each one. Put her bare palm flat on a frosted car roof for one second,
then back in the mitten.

> 👶 **Infant Integration:** Kreston is 8 months and stranger wariness has
> landed, so the far end of the table will not do. Sit him where he can see
> your face while you write, with a wooden spoon and a yoghurt pot to bang
> together. Outside, hold his bare palm on the frost for a second and watch his
> face.

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

### 🎨 Second Main Event: Her Page in the Cookbook

This afternoon her recipe becomes a page in a book with her name on the front.

**🧰 Complete Materials List:**
- Paper, 5–6 sheets
- The written recipe from this morning
- Crayons or felt tips
- Tape or glue
- 1 cereal box cut flat for the cover
- String and a hole punch, or a stapler
- A permanent marker

**📝 Step-by-Step Instructions:**
1. Trim the sheets to a size her hands can turn and lay them in order.
2. Tape the morning's recipe onto the middle page, butter stain and crossings-out and all.
3. She draws one ingredient per sheet on the pages before it: butter, sugar, egg, flour, her own addition. Write the word underneath in your own hand, big.
4. Cut the cereal box flat for the cover and let her colour it. Ask her the name of the book and write it across the front word for word.
5. Punch two holes down the left edge of every sheet and thread the string. Three staples down the spine work as well.
6. Read it back cover to cover, turning the pages at her pace.
7. Stand it on the kitchen shelf with the real cookbooks, at her height.

> 💡 **Tip:** She matches the shape of the word to the picture she made, and by Thursday she will find *butter* on the page unprompted.

### 🎯 Afternoon Alternatives
- One page only, her drawing on one side and her words on the other.
- Photograph each step of the morning and stick the photos in instead.
- Add a page for a recipe she invents that nobody has to make.

### 🌳 Out Again: **The Museum**
- **The museum**, first Saturday of February and the second of the year
- Soft play, if the car park beats you
- The park, and a bag of yesterday's rolls if any survived
- A café, and she orders her own thing out loud
- The Saturday market, for a stall with something baked on it

> 👶 **Kreston's Afternoon:** Kreston goes in the carrier facing in today. At 8
> months a museum is a room full of unfamiliar faces at his eye level, and your
> chest is where he turns back to between looks. Find one low thing with lights
> or a mirror and sit on the floor while he works it. Twenty-five minutes is a
> full visit.

---

> 🧠 **A Little Parenting Insight**
> **Dictated stories are the bridge to real literacy.**
> Azlyn cannot write and she can compose. Those two come apart at three, and
> taking her recipe down word for word lets the second run ahead of the first.
> She watches her own speech turn into marks you read back the same way twice,
> which is the idea underneath all of reading: the marks hold the words.
> Teachers call it language experience, and children who dictate to an adult
> arrive at writing with more to say and less fear of a blank page. Keep her
> wrong grammar in. Her sentence made the trip onto paper whole and comes back
> the same.

> ⚠️ **Safety:** Raw egg and raw flour both carry a real infection risk and she
> will eat some of the mixture. Hold it to one small spoonful, or set a
> spoonful aside before the egg goes in. Kreston gets none of it raw, and
> biscuits are sugar all the way through, so he skips the baked ones too. The
> bowl goes in the sink and not on the floor.

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
